# Snapback 4 — agent command sheet

Read this, then [LLP 3000](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the root and
the bakeoff protocol), [LLP 2000.000](../llp/2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md)
(the design brief), [`NOT-DOING.md`](./NOT-DOING.md), and the tail of
[`JOURNAL.md`](./JOURNAL.md). `rules/RULES.md` at the repository root is
adopted here as written; LLPs for Snapback 4 are numbered **3000 and up**
(`llp-create --under 3000` for a child; extend LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000
before minting a new number).

```
cargo build                          # the binary; no JS engine, no bootstrap; crates.io plus one vendored patch (vendor/tiny_http)
node scripts/affected.mjs --base origin/main --worktree --run # landing: fast/affected; prints every reason and retains a receipt
scripts/test.sh                      # complete battery: schedule/on demand; packed qualification: publication
./target/debug/snapback4 --help      # init [--online|--expo] · check [--cost|--residency|--fix|--json] · dev · deploy · rollback · serve · run (an op, or a job as the system) · test · why · guide · data · sql · token (a service's seat) · doctor · device · types · --version
./target/debug/snapback4 guide       # lists cards (`guide aliases` lists aliases); `guide language` for the language card — every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite (crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs)
cd apps/chat && ../../target/debug/snapback4 check     # compiles snapback/*.q, writes snapback/generated/api.ts
cd apps/chat && ../../target/debug/snapback4 dev       # serves 127.0.0.1:4400; re-adopts the schema on save
cd apps/chat && bun run dev                            # the app on the real client (?as=bob picks a persona)
bun run --cwd packages/snapback4 typecheck   # the contract, the client and the hooks typecheck
cargo test -p snapback4-core --test corpus -- --nocapture   # the conformance corpus, with skip reasons
cargo test -p snapback4-difftest     # fixed seeds, oracle regressions and replay (opt-in, no database by default)
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- compile case.q   # a generated or hand-written source's binder diagnostics ("ok" when none)
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 6 --out /tmp/quarry-difftest
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/rewrite-identity--why--NOT_FOUND
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run-devices --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 8 --out /tmp/quarry-devices   # server against both device stores; no database
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/device-sqlite--write-predicted-refused--admitted
(cd lean && lake build) && cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- run-lean --seed 0 --cases 100000 --workers 8 --out /tmp/quarry-lean   # the engine against the Lean model (LLP 3000.035 §5.2); ~900 cases/s on 8 workers
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest -- replay 'crates/snapback4-difftest/cases/found/lean--kind--committed~NOT_FOUND'   # needs the model built, or SNAPBACK4_LEAN_ORACLE=<quarry-oracle>
./target/debug/snapback4 check --emit-lean schema.q   # one checked file as the Lean model's case JSON; programs outside its fragment listed with a reason
# Store tests require SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES pointing to a private evaluation cluster.
cargo test -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres  # fails if the cluster is absent
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres -- run-stores --seed 0 --cases 1000000 --workers 8 --seconds 10800 --out /tmp/quarry-stores
cargo run --release -p snapback4-difftest --features postgres -- replay /path/to/found-case
python3 scripts/test-difftest-scheduled.py  # runner/ticket tests; no schedule or push
scripts/difftest-scheduled.sh --checkout "$HOME/quarry-difftest-runner" --state-dir "$HOME/.snapback-quarry-difftest" --hours 3
# Operator only: install daily launchd (Darwin) or user systemd timer (Linux).
scripts/install-difftest-schedule.sh --checkout "$HOME/quarry-difftest-runner"
cargo test -p snapback4-sim          # the chat world under faults: the seven invariants
cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test exhaustive   # every sequence of two writes (two viewers, two groups): disclosure and write authority against a model of the rules
SNAPBACK4_EXHAUSTIVE_DEPTH=3 cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test exhaustive -- --ignored   # three writes: ~7,000 states, minutes
scripts/kani.sh [harness...]         # Kani proofs of the key encoding (crates/snapback4-core/src/key_proofs.rs; minutes, ~12 GB peak); outside test.sh
(cd lean && ./check.sh)              # the Lean model of Quarry's core (LLP 3000.035 §5.2): lake build (~15 s cold, needs elan), no-sorry/axiom audit, 53 examples, the quarry-oracle I/O fixture; outside test.sh; open points in lean/OPEN.md
cargo test -p snapback4-sim --test native   # native jobs: builds native-fixtures/rescore for wasm32-unknown-unknown (cold ~20 s, then cached under target/native-fixture) and runs it
bash scripts/build-wasm.sh           # the device as wasm into packages/snapback4/src/wasm/ (the web tier and the Chromium witness need it); fails when the gzip exceeds metrics/wasm-budget.json — a landing that needs the bytes raises the budget in the same commit, with the reason
node witness/idb.mjs                 # the Chromium witness: the client on the wasm device over IndexedDB (needs Chrome)
WITNESS_UDID=<dedicated-udid> node witness/expo.mjs   # published witness verb/helper; freshly install apps/chat-expo Release first (simctl uninstall a previous build: its store belongs to an earlier backend); port 4400, no Metro
node witness/scale.mjs [G] [N] [M]   # cold sync at scale: G groups × N messages × M members into the wasm device; wall time + JS heap → metrics/witness.jsonl
node scripts/measure-storage.mjs     # what the store weighs on the scale seed (rows, indexes, partition log) → metrics/storage.jsonl
                                     # SNAPBACK4_BIN=target/release/snapback4 measures the release build (throughput numbers)
node scripts/measure-client.mjs      # each client tier's bytes on the pinned toolchain → metrics/client.jsonl
node scripts/attribute-wasm.mjs [--families] [--check-families] [--check-absent] # class bytes and concrete serde families; --check-families refuses non-text readers/non-Vec writers outside the exact dynamic-envelope, counting-writer and Scan map-replay allowlists; content-buffer families name the duplicated types and refuse Deserialize representations using untagged, tag or flatten; --check-absent refuses the core's prefetch, admission or log-event symbols in the web artifact (needs twiggy; slow, outside test.sh)
bash scripts/check-release-guard.sh # the web artifact's configuration plus the debug-only `web-device-check` feature must refuse to build (in test.sh)
bash scripts/measure-wasm.sh         # the device core as wasm (crates/snapback4-device), family-checked, bound and measured → metrics/wasm.jsonl
bash scripts/build-xcframework.sh    # the device as an iOS xcframework for packages/snapback4-expo (staticlib alone, stripped; 13.2 MB with native reads)
node loopbar/run.mjs --n 3           # the loop bar: fresh agents on the published pin build reduced Clique M1–M3 → metrics/loopbar.jsonl
node scripts/publish.mjs --prepare-client # rebuild wasm + clean client, pack once for cross-host qualification
# Linux qualification host: strace >=6.8 on PATH (audits every successful TCP listen).
node scripts/qualify-linux.mjs --client /path/client.tgz --expo /path/expo.tgz --out /path/qualification # Linux suite + Ubuntu/Alpine proof, no publish
# Add --consumer-only for packed frontend/toolchain proofs without test.sh; this writes consumer.json, never a publication receipt.
node scripts/publish.mjs             # Mac: qualify/sign Darwin; optional --linux-receipt + --linux-tarball import; platforms before client
```

Ordinary landings use `node snapback4/scripts/affected.mjs --base origin/main --run`
from the repository root; add `--worktree` for staged, unstaged and untracked
changes. `--json` prints the plan. Every command has a timeout, failures are
collected, and receipts live in `target/affected/`. Cargo runs with
`CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0 CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_DEBUG=0 CARGO_PROFILE_TEST_DEBUG=0`.
The selector reads Cargo's workspace graph, closes source changes over reverse
dependencies, narrows test-only changes to targets/files, and follows client
imports/process consumers. Unknown paths, locks/manifests, build scripts,
proc-macros and shared test support fail broad with a reason per file. The
complete battery belongs to the schedule; packed qualification belongs to
publication. An affected receipt does not qualify a release. See
[LLP 0189](../llp/0189-verification-cadence-and-claim-gating.decision.md#snapback-4-affected-selection-2026-10-24).

Crates: `snapback4-core` (value model, schema with `sync`, IR, key encoding,
SQLite store, rules with hoisting, the meter, the interpreter, the partition
log, at-most-once, the day-two classifier, wire forms), `snapback4-lang`
(Quarry lexer, parser, binder, diagnostics, the TypeScript emitter),
`snapback4` (verbs and the HTTP wire), `snapback4-sim` (the deterministic
world hosting the engine and a replica device), `snapback4-difftest` (seeded
Quarry source generator, rewrite and meter oracles, optional Postgres store
oracles with prefetch on/off, the server-against-device oracle on both device stores, the engine-against-Lean-model oracle, structural shrinking and replay; not published or linked by shipped crates), `snapback4-device` (the
interpreter over a `DeviceStore`: the core's SQLite under the native
module for Expo and Exact2 hosts, memory under wasm on the web; one
dispatcher, one suite on both)). The guide (`snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs`) is
the language card and the keyword cards; packages: `snapback4` (the client
over the device and the CLI shim), `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and `snapback4-linux-x64` (the binaries), `snapback4-expo`
(the Swift module over the device's C ABI).

What exists, by week (LLP 3000 §5):

| Week | Lands |
|---|---|
| 1 | the contract (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`), the mocked chat app against it, the hub fixture wrapper, the five floor measurements in the journal, the simulator skeleton (`crates/snapback4-sim`), the Snapback 2 corpus copied in, 0.0.1 published |
| 2 | server: SQLite transaction and interpreter, rules and uniformity, the meter, the partition log (`sync`), generations, personas, the wire, `check`/`dev`/`run`/`test`/`why`/`guide`, the seven simulator invariants |
| 3 | web client: replica store, outbox with client-minted ids, local interpreter, reactive local queries, the online-only tier, the chat app on the real client |
| 4 | Expo client, day-two scenarios, deploy, the loop bar, the Clique hub run, the comparison record |

Gotchas you cannot derive from the code:

- Own Cargo and Bun workspaces; the repository root excludes them. Run cargo/bun from `snapback4/`. Node 24.x (`engines`): run the suite with the pinned runtime on PATH, not the ambient Homebrew node.
- Web builds require `bun install --frozen-lockfile` first. Binaryen 132.0.0 is
  an exact workspace devDependency; build/measurement invoke its portable
  `node_modules/binaryen/bin/wasm-opt` through Node, never a PATH optimizer.
  Missing/mismatched dependencies and optimizer failures refuse the build.
  See README's web-device build instructions and HV round 4 for host parity.
- The suite schedules client files in one pool: `max(1, min(24,
  floor(os.availableParallelism() / 3)))` slots (3 on 10 CPUs, 24 on 96).
  This counts Linux CPU affinity and leaves room for each file's servers,
  browser renderers and workers. Acquisition and server-e2e share that pool;
  there are no extra shard processes outside it. Real-timer files listed in
  `TIMER_CLIENT_FILES` drain that pool and run individually afterward with
  unchanged timing bounds. The quote-jump browser witness remains pooled.
  The two full-count model
  files retain a separate serial slot. `SNAPBACK4_TEST_SERIAL_CLIENT_FILES=1`
  still forces one client file at a time. The external file watchdog measures
  time since a subtest started or finished: max(900 seconds, largest single
  declared guard + max(60 seconds, 25%)). There is no absolute duration cap.
  Console output does not count as progress. Serial mode uses the same bounds.
  See `scripts/run-client-tests.mjs` and the QF round-2 guard census; update
  named work counts and recheck the census when declarations change. Every
  file runs as native `node --test` with default isolation, in a process group
  whose first member is a tiny Node keeper. The runner closes the keeper's stdin
  on completion, stall or abort; only the keeper calls `kill(0, SIGKILL)`.
  Runner death also closes that pipe. An unexpectedly exited keeper fails the
  file and never authorizes a runner signal. Cleanup requires two consecutive
  completed inspections (50 ms apart) with no living group member. Observed leftovers
  fail as TEST_FILE_LEAK. Failed listings and unclassifiable PID/PPID/PGID
  prefixes remain fail-closed. A ps timeout at two seconds retries once with a
  60-second bound; all other command errors refuse immediately. Listings use
  asynchronous spawn so other files' timers and keeper EOF delivery keep running.
  File watchdogs and cleanup's five-second window use per-file monotonic time.
  Expiry requires another completed snapshot: a live group after the deadline
  refuses; two consecutive empty snapshots pass even when the second is late.
  Precisely
  recognized exit-time routing anomalies
  discard the whole snapshot and retry at most twice, 50 ms apart; persistent
  anomalies refuse and never confer ownership (LLP 3000 §7, QF2).
  Metadata is validated for group members and
  their visible descendants; unrelated rows only increment diagnostics.
  Signed Darwin UIDs, variable lstart spacing and comm spaces are accepted.
  Vanished members count as exited. Processes leaving the group (setsid,
  setpgid or detached spawns) are outside cleanup custody; visible descendants
  receive metadata validation but are never signalled by the runner. Native Node's exit
  status alone decides test success; reporter counts are diagnostic only.
  The reporter observes progress as Node delivers it: synchronous work that
  does not yield may buffer events in Node's child until that work finishes.
  Such an unreported interval is subject to the same stall bound. See QF Round 7.
  The M5 MacBook is the publication host; the office M1 is calibration-only.
- Client tests and their helpers must gate Linux APIs (`/proc`, `strace`, `ss`,
  `taskset`, namespace/network tools) at the test or call, or implement a
  portable fallback. Keep portable assertions running on Darwin and give each
  skipped witness an explicit reason. The client pool runs
  `test-platform-audit.test.mjs`: a cheap static census follows static named/namespace `.mjs`
  imports and named helpers and checks scoped Linux skips/branches/early returns.
  A gate in another test does not count. This conservative common-case guard
  does not prove dynamic imports, generated scripts or arbitrary control flow;
  review those paths and run affected tests on the publication Mac.
  Listener snapshots and syscall tracing are Linux qualification capabilities,
  never dependencies of the published `snapback4/proof` browser helper.
- Before a publication dry run, warm the test artifacts **in that publication
  worktree**, while the host is free of calibration work:
  `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS="-DHAVE_FDATASYNC=1 -USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT" cargo test --workspace --no-run`.
  Use the dry run's toolchain, target directory, profile/features, `RUSTFLAGS`
  and Cargo profile environment. Do not add `--release` or `--profile production`:
  the suite uses the default test profile. The suite still asks Cargo for the
  complete test inventory, runs every binary and runs doctests separately.
  Warming compiles without executing tests and supplies no qualification receipt.
- One owner on the core (LLP 3000 decision 4): the engine, the store and the client contract are written by the lead session; Codex lanes take the fixture generator, the reference apps, the device witnesses and the periphery. Do not reshape `contract.ts` from a lane; propose the change in the journal.
- No ceiling is raised in slice one (LLP 2000.000 §12). A thing that does not fit changes the design or waits.
- The journal is append-only and dated; never rewrite an entry. LLP 3000 §7 carries decisions that must outlive it.
- Nothing here depends on `snapback2/` at runtime. Copy source deliberately, split under 1,500 lines on the way in, and say so in the journal.
- Named lane: `~/projects/snapback-sb4` on `sb4/*` branches. Never `git stash`; commit checkpoints.
- The conformance corpus (`corpus/`) is Snapback 2's serialized IR and value
  model; the core keeps those serializations so it binds. A case the slice
  cannot run is skipped with a named reason, never counted as a pass.
- `snapback/generated/api.ts` is built by `check` and `dev`, never committed;
  the app's typecheck needs a `check` first (the suite does one).
- Point rule probes and `exists` require a unique key. A fully pinned superset
  of a required unique key is unique too (for example an explicit role index
  containing the unique conversation/principal pair). Nullable unique key
  arguments need non-null proof. Other existence checks use bounded populations
  in code. Named equalities covering a total unique key check extra columns on
  that one row without an additional index.
- The packaged CLI uses `LIBSQLITE3_FLAGS="-DHAVE_FDATASYNC=1 -USQLITE_ENABLE_MEMORY_MANAGEMENT"` during both publication tests and its production build (LLP 3000 §7; fdatasync per LLP 3000.009.001.000.002 "SQLite durable write cycle"). `snapback4/.cargo/config.toml` supplies the same default to Cargo runs started inside `snapback4/`; it does not override an inherited variable, and a Cargo run started from the repository root does not read it; use the same build input when comparing with the package. The variable is unnecessary at runtime.
- When creating a stage, `scripts/publish.mjs` runs the suite and refuses on red, builds the production binary, packs `snapback4-darwin-arm64` and verifies the installed digest, then publishes both packages. Bump `version` in `packages/snapback4/package.json` (and its `optionalDependencies`), `packages/snapback4-darwin-arm64/package.json`,
  `packages/snapback4-linux-x64/package.json` and `Cargo.toml` together; a test refuses a mismatch.
- Publication retains exact qualified tarballs under `target/publication/`. If a
  stage exists, use `node scripts/publish.mjs --resume` to validate its recorded
  suite qualification, requalify those exact bytes through the installed-consumer
  proof, and finish missing registry artifacts; an existing version must match
  its staged integrity. This also applies after `--dry-run`. An ordinary invocation
  refuses an existing stage so changed source cannot silently publish old bytes.
  Bump the version to build a new pair; preserve a partially published stage.
- Cross-host publication (LLP 3000.021 §3.3): prepare the common client once
  from clean release source, qualify that tarball on Linux, and import the
  Linux tarball plus its receipt on the Mac. Source commit, client SRI and
  Linux SRI must agree. Linux prefers working Docker/Podman, otherwise uses checksum-pinned proot
---
name: llp-orient
description: Before editing a subsystem, file, or starting a task, assemble the governing LLP context — read the root LLP, follow the @ref annotations on the code in scope, and find the LLPs covering the relevant systems. Produces a compact context pack of the constraints a change must respect. Read-only.
source: ccheever/llp@v0.5.2
---

# llp-orient

<!-- @ref https://github.com/ccheever/llp/blob/v0.5.2/llp/0008-distributed-agent-skills.rfc.md#the-skills--core-five-plus-flags — skill contract -->
<!-- @ref https://github.com/ccheever/llp/blob/v0.5.2/llp/0000-linked-literate-programming.explainer.md#2-reference-syntax — the grammar this reads -->

Load the design rationale governing the area you're about to touch, before touching it. This is the consumption side of LLP — orienting first is what turns `@ref` from a possibility into a habit.

Invoke as `/llp-orient <path-or-task>` (file, directory, subsystem, or task description). With no argument, use the current change scope (`git diff --name-only`).

## Trigger

Start of a coding task that touches an area with documented design. The orient-first *rule* lives in the repo's always-loaded `AGENTS.md`; this skill is the detailed form that rule routes to for non-trivial work — don't rely on ambient description-match alone.

## Invariants

- MUST be read-only: never edit code or LLP documents.
- MUST scope to the task: pull the constraints that bind this change, not a corpus summary.
- MUST cite, not restate: reference policy as `LLP NNNN#anchor` rather than paraphrasing sections.
- MUST NOT invent constraints. If something isn't documented, say so.
- MUST NOT guess the rationale behind a broken `@ref` — report it and hand off to `llp-maintain`.

## Workflow

> **Recipe (advisory)** — corpus root = nearest `llp/` walking up from the path in scope (LLP 0001 §Unit of adoption); "no corpus" is an empty walk, not cwd-relative absence. Read `llp/foundation/` first (the kernel; the root LLP is there once `Active` — on a fresh adoption it is still `Draft` and sits in `current/`), then skim `llp/current/` (the declared working set); both hold relative links — symlinks, or small files containing the relative path — so `ls` answers and either form resolves. Then the root LLP's systems vocabulary. `Grep '@ref'` over the files in scope and `Read` each target — these are the highest-signal context. `Grep` the `**Systems:**` headers across `llp/` for LLPs governing the subsystems touched; read the governing ones (Spec, Decision, Principles, relevant RFCs). Follow `Related:` and nearby `@ref` targets one hop out; stop there unless something is clearly load-bearing. Stale-pin nudge (skip offline): read the installed tag from the skills receipt (or a `source:` pin), compare `git ls-remote --tags <origin>` — if upstream is newer, say so in one line ("skills at v0.3.0, upstream v0.5.2 — run /llp-adopt update") and move on; never update from here.

## Artifact

A context pack with these sections, faster to read than the documents it points at:

- **Scope** — files / subsystems queried
- **Governing LLPs** — numbered list, one-line gloss each; mark which are in `foundation/` and which are in `current/` (in-flight — its guidance may still move)
- **Active constraints** — bullets, each citing `LLP NNNN#anchor`
- **Relevant rationale** — the *why*, same citation form
- **Open questions / risks** for this area
- **Likely doc updates** if the change lands
- **Hand-off** — one of `none | llp-adopt | llp-create | llp-review | llp-maintain`

## Hand-offs

- No corpus (no `llp/` walking up from the path in scope) → `llp-adopt`.
- The task needs a decision no LLP covers → `llp-create` (or `llp-review` if proposal-shaped).
- An `@ref` target or anchor doesn't resolve → `llp-maintain`.

// Shared with browser bundles; inactive outside proof runs. Never retains
// arguments, tokens, row data, messages or response bodies.
export interface RefusalObservation { operation: string; role: string; code: string; field: string }
let enabled = false;
const sinkKey = Symbol.for("snapback4.proof.refusal");
const sink = () => (globalThis as Record<symbol, unknown>)[sinkKey] as ((event: RefusalObservation) => void) | undefined;
const events: RefusalObservation[] = [];
const observed = new WeakMap<object, RefusalObservation>();
export function refusalObservation(value: object): RefusalObservation | undefined { return observed.get(value); }
const roles = new Map<string, string>();
const label = (value: unknown, fallback: string) => typeof value === 'string' && /^[\w.:/ -]{1,180}$/.test(value) ? value : fallback;
export function enableProofObservation(): void { enabled = true; }
export function proofRole(token: string, role: string): void {
  if (roles.size >= 256) roles.delete(roles.keys().next().value!);
  roles.set(token, label(role, 'authenticated'));
}
export function proofRoleName(token?: string): string { return token ? roles.get(token) ?? 'authenticated (role unavailable)' : 'anonymous'; }
export function proofEvents(): readonly RefusalObservation[] { return events; }
export function observeRefusal(operation: string, role: string, value: unknown): void {
  if ((!enabled && !sink()) || !value || typeof value !== 'object') return;
  const why = value as {code?:unknown;site?:unknown};
  if (typeof why.code !== 'string' || !/^[A-Z][A-Z0-9_]{1,80}$/.test(why.code)) return;
  const event = { operation: label(operation, 'operation unavailable'), role: label(role, 'role unavailable'), code: why.code,
    field: label(why.site, 'field unavailable') };
  try { sink()?.(event); } catch {}
  observed.set(value, event);
  events.push(event);
  if (events.length > 32) events.shift();
}
export function observeProofReply(url: string, headers: HeadersInit | undefined, value: unknown): void {
  if (!enabled) return;
  let path: string; try { path = new URL(url).pathname; } catch { return; }
  const h = new Headers(headers);
  const token = h.get('authorization')?.replace(/^Bearer /, '');
  const role = h.get('x-snapback-persona') ?? (token ? roles.get(token) ?? 'authenticated (role unavailable)' : 'anonymous');
  if (!value || typeof value !== 'object') return;
  const v = value as {denied?:unknown;why?:unknown};
  observeRefusal(path.replace(/^\/[qma]\//, ''), role, v.denied ?? v.why);
}
export async function proofResponse(input: Parameters<typeof fetch>[0], init: RequestInit | undefined, response: Response): Promise<void> {
  if (!enabled) return;
  try {
    const url = typeof input === 'string' ? input : input instanceof URL ? input.href : input.url;
    const path = new URL(url).pathname;
    if (!/^\/[qma]\//.test(path) && !path.startsWith('/assets')) return;
    const h = new Headers(init?.headers);
    const token = h.get('authorization')?.replace(/^Bearer /, '');
    const role = h.get('x-snapback-persona') ?? (token ? roles.get(token) ?? 'authenticated (role unavailable)' : 'anonymous');
    const operation = path.replace(/^\/[qma]\//, '');
    const json = response.json.bind(response);
    // Observe the caller's one decode, preserving the exact returned object.
    // No cloned streams, eager body reads, retained credentials or extra work.
    response.json = async () => {
      const value: unknown = await json();
      try {
        if (value && typeof value === 'object') {
          const v = value as {denied?:unknown;why?:unknown};
          observeRefusal(operation, role, v.denied ?? v.why);
        }
      } catch { /* Diagnostics cannot replace a result. */ }
      return value;
    };
  } catch { /* Frozen/custom response objects keep their original behavior. */ }
}

import { openSync, closeSync, readSync, fstatSync } from 'node:fs';
import { enableProofObservation, proofEvents, refusalObservation, type RefusalObservation } from './proof-observation.ts';
const servers = new Set<string>();
let closedServerEvent: string | undefined;
const reported = new WeakSet<object>();
export function proofServerLog(path: string): void { servers.add(path); }
export function forgetProofServerLog(path: string): void { closedServerEvent = lastServerEvent() ?? closedServerEvent; servers.delete(path); }
const fixes: Record<string, string> = {
  E_INPUT: 'match the named argument/field to its generated signature; snapback4 guide api generated',
  E_ASSET_UNKNOWN: 'await client.upload, check result.ok, then pass result.asset.id from this backend/user; snapback4 guide uploads',
  E_ASSET_TYPE: 'upload verified bytes accepted by the destination column; snapback4 guide uploads',
  E_AUTH: 'sign in to this backend and use that user session; snapback4 guide auth',
  E_ADMISSION: 'use createApp with a writable session store before browser mutations; snapback4 guide client',
  E_RULE: 'check this role and the named write rule against the intended operation; snapback4 guide rules',
};
function eventIn(error: unknown, seen = new Set<object>()): RefusalObservation | undefined {
  if (!error || typeof error !== "object" || seen.has(error)) return;
  seen.add(error);
  const found = refusalObservation(error); if (found) return found;
  const e = error as {actual?:unknown;why?:unknown;denied?:unknown;cause?:unknown};
  return eventIn(e.actual,seen) ?? eventIn(e.why,seen) ?? eventIn(e.denied,seen) ?? eventIn(e.cause,seen);
}
function refusalIn(error: unknown, seen = new Set<object>()): string | undefined {
  if (!error || typeof error !== 'object') return;
  if (seen.has(error)) return; seen.add(error);
  const e = error as {actual?:unknown;why?:unknown;denied?:unknown;code?:unknown;cause?:unknown};
  if (typeof e.code === 'string' && /^E_[A-Z0-9_]+$/.test(e.code)) return e.code;
  return refusalIn(e.actual, seen) ?? refusalIn(e.why, seen) ?? refusalIn(e.denied, seen) ?? refusalIn(e.cause, seen);
}
function lastServerEvent(): string | undefined {
  for (const path of [...servers].reverse()) {
    let fd: number | undefined;
    try {
      fd = openSync(path, 'r'); const size = fstatSync(fd).size;
      const buffer = Buffer.alloc(Math.min(size, 16384)); readSync(fd, buffer, 0, buffer.length, Math.max(0, size-buffer.length));
      const lines = buffer.toString().split('\n');
      for (const line of lines.reverse()) {
        const code = line.match(/\b(E_[A-Z0-9_]+)\b/)?.[1];
        if (code) return `nearest server event: ${code}; inspect private server log ${path}`;
        if (/\b(error|panic|failed)\b/i.test(line)) return `nearest server error: inspect private server log ${path}`;
      }
    } catch {} finally { if (fd !== undefined) closeSync(fd); }
  }
}
/** Content only: runners own the layout/budget. Expected refusal assertions
 * remain unchanged. An observed request is context, never proof of causality.
 * @throws None for native Error/AssertionError inputs; unavailable logs are ignored. */
export function proofFailure(error: unknown, observations: readonly RefusalObservation[] = proofEvents()): string {
  const code = refusalIn(error);
  const exact = eventIn(error);
  const event = exact ?? (code ? [...observations].reverse().find(event => event.code === code) : observations.at(-1));
  if (event) return `proof refusal${exact ? '' : ' (nearest observed request)'}: operation ${event.operation}; role ${event.role}; ${event.code}; field ${event.field}\nlikely fix: ${fixes[event.code] ?? 'inspect the named field and authored guard; snapback4 guide why ' + event.code}`;
  if (code) return `proof refusal: ${code}; operation/role/field unavailable\nlikely fix: ${fixes[code] ?? 'inspect the refusal and its authored guard; snapback4 guide why ' + code}`;
  return lastServerEvent() ?? closedServerEvent ?? 'proof failed: no backend refusal or server error observed; inspect the assertion below';
}
/** Print refusal content once, preserving the error. @throws Output stream write failures. */
export function reportProofFailure(error: unknown): void {
  if (error && typeof error === 'object') { if (reported.has(error)) return; reported.add(error); }
  console.error(proofFailure(error));
}
// Top-level starter assertions retain Node's class, exit status, stack and diff.
let installed = false;
export function installProofFailureReporting(): void {
  enableProofObservation();
  if (installed) return; installed = true;
  process.on('uncaughtExceptionMonitor', error => { reportProofFailure(error); });
}

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/repair/safe.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/repair/safe.rs

index 0000000000..88cbfda84b
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/repair/safe.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,165 @@
+//! Conservative, context-compiled fix-its. No invented defaults, bounds or rules.
+use super::*;
+use crate::ast::Expr as A;
+
+// @ref LLP 3000.007#8 — a fix must be a verified source edit, never prose advice.
+pub(super) fn verify(documents: &[Document], diagnostics: &mut [Diagnostic]) {
+    for d in diagnostics {
+        if !matches!(d.code.as_str(), "E_QUARRY_TYPE" | "E_QUARRY_UNKNOWN" | "E_QUARRY_BOUND") { continue; }
+        let Some(doc) = documents.iter().find(|doc| doc.file == d.site.file) else { continue; };
+        let Some((expr, text, _, start)) = doc.source.expressions.get(&d.site.to_string()).or_else(||
+            doc.source.expressions.values().filter(|(expr, ..)| expr_at(expr, &d.site).is_some()).min_by_key(|(_,text,..)| text.len())) else { continue; };
+        let Some(offending) = expr_at(expr, &d.site) else { continue; };
+        // Never edit table/column rules, invariants, or other security declarations.
+        let Some(program) = doc.programs.iter().filter(|p| p.site.line <= start.line)
+            .max_by_key(|p| p.site.line) else { continue; };
+        let Some(source) = doc.source.declarations.get(&program.site.to_string()) else { continue; };
+        let mut edit_start = start.clone();
+        let mut original = text.clone();
+        let rewrite = if d.message.contains("needs a bool condition, got bool ?") {
+            Some(format!("({text}) = true"))
+        } else if d.message.contains("needs present numbers; got ") || d.message.contains("needs present operands; got ") {
+            if let Some((condition, guards)) = guarded_condition(&program.body, offending, &d.message, doc) {
+                if let Some((_, value, _, at)) = doc.source.expressions.get(&condition.site().to_string()) {
+                    original = value.clone(); edit_start = at.clone();
+                    Some(format!("{} and ({value})", guards.join(" and ")))
+                } else { None }
+            } else if expr.site() == &d.site { nullable_arithmetic(expr, text, &d.message, doc) } else { None }
+        } else if d.message.starts_with("insert into ") && d.message.contains("omits required column") {
+            insert(expr, text, program, &d.message)
+        } else if d.code == "E_QUARRY_UNKNOWN" && d.message.starts_with("unknown name ") {
+            // Only a unique case-insensitive parameter spelling: never guess a
+            // similar role, table, helper, enum or authorization expression.
+            if let A::Name { name, .. } = expr {
+                let names: Vec<_> = program.args.iter().filter(|a| a.name != *name && a.name.eq_ignore_ascii_case(name)).collect();
+                if let [arg] = names.as_slice() { Some(arg.name.clone()) } else { None }
+            } else { None }
+        } else if d.code == "E_QUARRY_BOUND" && total_unique(expr, documents) {
+            // A unique, fully pinned non-null key preserves every row with first 1.
+            d.rewrite.split_once("makes this at most one row: `").and_then(|(_, s)| s.split_once('`')).map(|(s, _)| s.to_owned())
+        } else { None };
+        let Some(rewrite) = rewrite else { continue; };
+        let Some(from) = offset(source, edit_start.line - program.site.line + 1, edit_start.col) else { continue; };
+        let span = from..from + original.len();
+        if source.get(span.clone()) != Some(original.as_str()) { continue; }
+        let candidate = Candidate { site: d.site.clone(), declaration_site: program.site.clone(), source: source.clone(), replacement: span,
+            rewrite, intended: None, message: None };
+        if check_context(documents, &candidate, false) != Check::Accepted { continue; }
+        d.rewrite = candidate.rewrite;
+        d.mechanical = true;
+        d.verified_edit = Some((edit_start.clone(), original.clone()));
+        // The diagnostic shows the exact edited source line, including its
+        // surrounding return/require/write, while rewrite stays the span edit.
+        if !original.contains('\n') && !d.rewrite.contains('\n') {
+            let line = source.lines().nth((edit_start.line - program.site.line) as usize).unwrap_or("");
+            if let Some(col) = offset(line, 1, edit_start.col) {
+                let mut edited = line.to_owned();
+                if edited.get(col..col + original.len()) == Some(original.as_str()) {
+                    edited.replace_range(col..col + original.len(), &d.rewrite);
+                    d.details = Some(format!("edited line: {edited}"));
+                }
+            }
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+fn offset(source: &str, line: u32, col: u32) -> Option<usize> {
+    let mut at = 0;
+    for (i, text) in source.split_inclusive('\n').enumerate() {
+        if i + 1 == line as usize { return text.char_indices().map(|(n, _)| n).chain([text.len()]).nth(col.checked_sub(1)? as usize).map(|n| at + n); }
+        at += text.len();
+    }
+    None
+}
+fn stable(expr: &A) -> bool { matches!(expr, A::Name { .. } | A::Literal { .. }) || matches!(expr, A::Member {base, ..} if matches!(base.as_ref(), A::Name {..})) }
+fn nullable_arithmetic(expr: &A, text: &str, message: &str, doc: &Document) -> Option<String> {
+    let A::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = expr else { return None; };
+    if !matches!(op.as_str(), "+" | "-" | "*") || !stable(left) || !stable(right) { return None; }
+    let (a, b) = message.split_once("got ")?.1.split_once(" and ")?;
+    let mut guards = Vec::new();
+    for (operand, ty) in [(left, a), (right, b)] {
+        if ty.ends_with(" ?") { guards.push(format!("{} != null", doc.source.expression(operand))); }
+    }
+    if guards.is_empty() { return None; }
+    // Null propagation retains absence. Choosing zero/now could admit a write
+    // that should refuse, so a required destination receives advice only.
+    Some(format!("({text} if {} else null)", guards.join(" and ")))
+}
+fn insert(expr: &A, text: &str, program: &ProgramDecl, message: &str) -> Option<String> {
+    let A::Write { op: WriteOp::Insert, condition: None, .. } = expr else { return None; };
+    let missing = message.split_once("omits required column")?.1.trim_start_matches('s').trim();
+    let names: Vec<_> = missing.split(", ").collect();
+    if names.iter().any(|name| !program.args.iter().any(|arg| arg.name == *name)) { return None; }
+    // Last brace is the insert payload (condition is excluded above). Preserve
+    // expressions, strings and comments verbatim; refuse commented tails.
+    let end = text.rfind('}')?;
+    let before = text[..end].trim_end();
+    if before.lines().last()?.contains("--") { return None; }
+    let comma = if before.ends_with(['{', ',']) { "" } else { "," };
+    Some(format!("{before}{comma} {} {}", names.join(", "), &text[end..]))
+}
+
+// Only a positive comparison/arithmetic condition. Do not move null guards
+// across not/or, calls, references, or unrelated branches.
+fn positive_contains(condition: &A, site: &Site) -> bool {
+    if condition.site() == site { return true; }
+    match condition {
+        A::Binary {op, left, right, ..} if matches!(op.as_str(), "+"|"-"|"*"|"<"|"<="|">"|">="|"="|"!="|"and") => positive_contains(left, site) || positive_contains(right, site),
+        _ => false,
+    }
+}
+fn guarded_condition<'a>(body: &'a [Stmt], expr: &A, message: &str, doc: &Document) -> Option<(&'a A, Vec<String>)> {
+    let A::Binary {left, right, ..} = expr else { return None; };
+    let (a,b) = message.split_once("got ")?.1.split_once(" and ")?;
+    let mut guards = Vec::new();
+    for (value, ty) in [(left, a), (right,b)] {
+        if ty.ends_with(" ?") { if !stable(value) { return None; } guards.push(format!("{} != null", doc.source.expression(value))); }
+    }
+    if guards.is_empty() { return None; }
+    for statement in body {
+        match statement {
+            Stmt::Require {condition,..} | Stmt::If {condition,..} if positive_contains(condition, expr.site()) => return Some((condition,guards)),
+            Stmt::If {then_body,else_body,..} => if let Some(found) = guarded_condition(then_body,expr,message,doc).or_else(||guarded_condition(else_body,expr,message,doc)) {return Some(found);},
+            _ => {},
+        }
+    }
+    None
+}
+
+#[cfg(test)]
+mod tests {
+    #[test]
+    fn nullable_unique_population_is_never_repaired_with_first_one() {
+        let source = "table tags:\n  slug: text <=40 ?\n  unique bySlug: slug\n  public\nquery q():\n  return tags[slug = null] by .slug\n";
+        let bound = crate::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);
+        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_BOUND"), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
+        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().all(|d| !d.mechanical), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    }
+    #[test]
+    fn nullable_guard_has_an_exact_source_span() {
+        let source = "query q(at: time ?):\n  require at + 1 > now else LATE\n  return 1\n";
+        let (doc, _) = crate::parse_document("schema.q", source);
+        let bound = crate::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);
+        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().all(|d| d.mechanical), "{:?}\n{:?}", bound.diagnostics, doc.source);
+    }
+}
+
+fn expr_at<'a>(expr: &'a A, site: &Site) -> Option<&'a A> {
+    if expr.site() == site { return Some(expr); }
+    match expr {
+        A::Binary {left,right,..} => expr_at(left,site).or_else(||expr_at(right,site)),
+        A::Unary {value,..} => expr_at(value,site),
+        A::Member {base,..} => expr_at(base,site),
+        _ => None,
+    }
+}
+
+fn total_unique(expr: &A, documents: &[Document]) -> bool {
+    let A::Read(read) = expr else { return false; };
+    let Some(table) = documents.iter().flat_map(|doc| &doc.tables).find(|table| table.name == read.table) else { return false; };
+    let named = |column: &str| read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.as_deref() == Some(column));
+    if named("id") { return true; }
+    table.indexes.iter().any(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.columns.len() == read.keys.len()
+        && index.columns.iter().all(|column| (column == "id" || table.columns.iter().any(|c| c.name == *column && !c.type_.optional))
+            && (named(column) || read.index.as_ref() == Some(&index.name) && read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_none()))))
+}

 }
 
+fn show_diff(name: &str, before: &str, after: &str) {
+    let (old, new): (Vec<_>, Vec<_>) = (before.lines().collect(), after.lines().collect());
+    let prefix = old.iter().zip(&new).take_while(|(a, b)| a == b).count();
+    let suffix = old[prefix..].iter().rev().zip(new[prefix..].iter().rev()).take_while(|(a, b)| a == b).count();
+    eprintln!("--- {name}\n+++ {name}\n@@ line {} @@", prefix + 1);
+    for line in &old[prefix..old.len() - suffix] { eprintln!("-{line}"); }
+    for line in &new[prefix..new.len() - suffix] { eprintln!("+{line}"); }
+}
+
 fn tokens(text: &str) -> Vec<Token> {
     lexer::lex("", text).0.into_iter().filter(|t| matches!(t.kind, Kind::Word | Kind::Number | Kind::Str | Kind::Symbol)).collect()
 }

     }
 }
+
+#[test]
+fn wave34_nullable_bool_time_missing_insert_and_parameter_case_resolve_in_one_fix_run() {
+    // Wave 34 E_QUARRY_TYPE/UNKNOWN: nullable times and bools, and required
+    // insert columns already present as arguments. No default values invented.
+    for (bad, good) in [
+        ("query q(flag: bool ?):\n  if flag:\n    return 1\n  return 0\n", "query q(flag: bool ?):\n  if (flag) = true:\n    return 1\n  return 0\n"),
+        ("query q(flag: bool ?):\n  require flag else FLAG\n  return 1\n", "query q(flag: bool ?):\n  require (flag) = true else FLAG\n  return 1\n"),
+        ("query q(at: time ?):\n  return at + 1 -- café\n", "query q(at: time ?):\n  return (at + 1 if at != null else null) -- café\n"),
+        ("query q(at: time ?):\n  require at + 1 > now else LATE\n  return 1\n", "query q(at: time ?):\n  require at != null and (at + 1 > now) else LATE\n  return 1\n"),
+        ("query q(Title: text <=20):\n  return title\n", "query q(Title: text <=20):\n  return Title\n"),
+        ("table notes:\n  title: text <=20\n  public\nmutation add(title: text <=20):\n  return insert notes {}\n", "table notes:\n  title: text <=20\n  public\nmutation add(title: text <=20):\n  return insert notes { title }\n"),
+    ] {
+        let output = check_fix(&[("schema.q", bad, good)], true);
+        assert!(String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).contains("--- schema.q\n+++ schema.q"));
+        let again = check_fix(&[("schema.q", good, good)], true);
+        assert!(!String::from_utf8_lossy(&again.stderr).contains("fix:"));
+    }
+}
+
+#[test]
+fn wave34_fix_never_invents_security_defaults_or_population_limits() {
+    for source in [
+        "table notes:\n  owner: principal\n  insert <- .owner = viewer\n  read <- .owner = viewer\nmutation add():\n  insert notes {}\n",
+        "table notes:\n  public\nquery q():\n  return notes\n",
+        "table notes:\n  at: time\n  public\nmutation add(at: time ?):\n  insert notes {at: at + 1}\n",
+        "table notes:\n  flag: bool ?\n  read <- .flag\n",
+    ] {
+        let sources = vec![("schema.q".into(), source.into())];
+        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&sources);
+        assert!(!bound.diagnostics.is_empty());
+        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().all(|d| !d.mechanical), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    }
+}

## 9. Wave-34 refusal and install calls (CALLS1)

The wave-34 decomposition attributes the remaining Codex input gap primarily
to extra model calls; nullable Quarry errors, image/file option confusion and
install polling are product-fixable sources. Muse acceptance assertions often
bury backend refusals. This increment changes diagnostics and tooling, not
rules or runtime language semantics.

`check --fix` applies only compiler-verified source spans. An optional Boolean
condition becomes an explicit comparison with true; null does not admit it.
Stable nullable arithmetic propagates null when the surrounding declaration
accepts that result. Positive require/if comparisons may instead guard the
optional operands before evaluating the original condition; an absent value
cannot satisfy the guard. Required insert columns may be filled only from
same-named declared arguments that pass the real binder in context. Case-only
parameter spelling repairs and a fully pinned, unique, non-null population's
`first 1` are similarly checked. No default zero/now, missing owner/role,
invented page size, schema cap, rule or invariant is introduced. Calls are not
duplicated by nullable arithmetic repairs. Unproved rewrites remain advice.
The CLI rechecks every compiled source before each write, prints a source diff,
recompiles after each edit and leaves a second run unchanged. Diagnostics show
the exact edited line for the new single-line repairs.

Proof failure content lives in `proofFailure`, callable by runner summaries.
Proof-enabled SDK transports retain at most 32 refusal records containing only
operation, role, code and field identifiers. Credentials, arguments, messages
and row contents are not retained. A roster maps an active token to its role
without printing it. Browser network replies and local admission refusals feed
the same formatter. Uncaught starter failures print its content before Node's
original assertion/stack/diff; browser callbacks report it before cleanup.
Assertions retain their identity, class and comparison fields. Exact decoded
refusal objects correlate with their request through a weak map. Code equality
alone never establishes request identity; without that correlation, a recent
observed request is explicitly labelled as context. Existing npm acceptance,
frontend:proof and client:calls scripts activate reporting even if they import
only the Node client. Device admission refusals retain their known operation
and the available persona or roster role. If no request refusal exists,
the nearest server refusal/error is named from the owned private log, without
printing raw server text. Missing operation/role/field evidence is stated rather
than invented. Runners retain ownership of output layout and budgets.

File upload options on a schema without file columns receive one narrowly
bounded retry after the explicit pre-body no-file-column refusal. The original
bytes then pass the existing media sniffer, image/video schema bounds and
normal authorization/placement checks. A mixed/file schema keeps explicit file
semantics. No transport ambiguity or post-staging refusal authorizes this
fallback; durable upload intents retain their immutable declared kind. Finite
security campaign output calls file scenarios inapplicable on image-only
schemas rather than displaying their expected refusal as a product red.

The first install remains `npm i snapback4`, exposing the guide in seconds.
Init prints the editable file map and reading route, then owns one blocking
frontend install. A fresh Codex replay showed tool cleanup reclaiming the
previous unjoined background owner before its first receipt; foreground
ownership removes that race. npm chatter stays in the private log, automatic
audit/funding chatter is deferred to the separate qualification path, and
completion preserves npm's exit status. An explicit `--no-install` still defers
installation; `node scripts/install.mjs --wait` joins a separately started
installer with one final line. The script's explicit background mode retains
request identity, IPC custody, owner-death cleanup and late-receipt checks.

index 0000000000..88cbfda84b
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/repair/safe.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,165 @@
+//! Conservative, context-compiled fix-its. No invented defaults, bounds or rules.
+use super::*;
+use crate::ast::Expr as A;
+
+// @ref LLP 3000.007#8 — a fix must be a verified source edit, never prose advice.
+pub(super) fn verify(documents: &[Document], diagnostics: &mut [Diagnostic]) {
+    for d in diagnostics {
+        if !matches!(d.code.as_str(), "E_QUARRY_TYPE" | "E_QUARRY_UNKNOWN" | "E_QUARRY_BOUND") { continue; }
+        let Some(doc) = documents.iter().find(|doc| doc.file == d.site.file) else { continue; };
+        let Some((expr, text, _, start)) = doc.source.expressions.get(&d.site.to_string()).or_else(||
+            doc.source.expressions.values().filter(|(expr, ..)| expr_at(expr, &d.site).is_some()).min_by_key(|(_,text,..)| text.len())) else { continue; };
+        let Some(offending) = expr_at(expr, &d.site) else { continue; };
+        // Never edit table/column rules, invariants, or other security declarations.
+        let Some(program) = doc.programs.iter().filter(|p| p.site.line <= start.line)
+            .max_by_key(|p| p.site.line) else { continue; };
+        let Some(source) = doc.source.declarations.get(&program.site.to_string()) else { continue; };
+        let mut edit_start = start.clone();
+        let mut original = text.clone();
+        let rewrite = if d.message.contains("needs a bool condition, got bool ?") {
+            Some(format!("({text}) = true"))
+        } else if d.message.contains("needs present numbers; got ") || d.message.contains("needs present operands; got ") {
+            if let Some((condition, guards)) = guarded_condition(&program.body, offending, &d.message, doc) {
+                if let Some((_, value, _, at)) = doc.source.expressions.get(&condition.site().to_string()) {
+                    original = value.clone(); edit_start = at.clone();
+                    Some(format!("{} and ({value})", guards.join(" and ")))
+                } else { None }
+            } else if expr.site() == &d.site { nullable_arithmetic(expr, text, &d.message, doc) } else { None }
+        } else if d.message.starts_with("insert into ") && d.message.contains("omits required column") {
+            insert(expr, text, program, &d.message)
+        } else if d.code == "E_QUARRY_UNKNOWN" && d.message.starts_with("unknown name ") {
+            // Only a unique case-insensitive parameter spelling: never guess a
+            // similar role, table, helper, enum or authorization expression.
+            if let A::Name { name, .. } = expr {
+                let names: Vec<_> = program.args.iter().filter(|a| a.name != *name && a.name.eq_ignore_ascii_case(name)).collect();
+                if let [arg] = names.as_slice() { Some(arg.name.clone()) } else { None }
+            } else { None }
+        } else if d.code == "E_QUARRY_BOUND" && total_unique(expr, documents) {
+            // A unique, fully pinned non-null key preserves every row with first 1.
+            d.rewrite.split_once("makes this at most one row: `").and_then(|(_, s)| s.split_once('`')).map(|(s, _)| s.to_owned())
+        } else { None };
+        let Some(rewrite) = rewrite else { continue; };
+        let Some(from) = offset(source, edit_start.line - program.site.line + 1, edit_start.col) else { continue; };
+        let span = from..from + original.len();
+        if source.get(span.clone()) != Some(original.as_str()) { continue; }
+        let candidate = Candidate { site: d.site.clone(), declaration_site: program.site.clone(), source: source.clone(), replacement: span,
+            rewrite, intended: None, message: None };
+        if check_context(documents, &candidate, false) != Check::Accepted { continue; }
+        d.rewrite = candidate.rewrite;
+        d.mechanical = true;
+        d.verified_edit = Some((edit_start.clone(), original.clone()));
+        // The diagnostic shows the exact edited source line, including its
+        // surrounding return/require/write, while rewrite stays the span edit.
+        if !original.contains('\n') && !d.rewrite.contains('\n') {
+            let line = source.lines().nth((edit_start.line - program.site.line) as usize).unwrap_or("");
+            if let Some(col) = offset(line, 1, edit_start.col) {
+                let mut edited = line.to_owned();
+                if edited.get(col..col + original.len()) == Some(original.as_str()) {
+                    edited.replace_range(col..col + original.len(), &d.rewrite);
+                    d.details = Some(format!("edited line: {edited}"));
+                }
+            }
+        }
+    }
+}
+
+fn offset(source: &str, line: u32, col: u32) -> Option<usize> {
+    let mut at = 0;
+    for (i, text) in source.split_inclusive('\n').enumerate() {
+        if i + 1 == line as usize { return text.char_indices().map(|(n, _)| n).chain([text.len()]).nth(col.checked_sub(1)? as usize).map(|n| at + n); }
+        at += text.len();
+    }
+    None
+}
+fn stable(expr: &A) -> bool { matches!(expr, A::Name { .. } | A::Literal { .. }) || matches!(expr, A::Member {base, ..} if matches!(base.as_ref(), A::Name {..})) }
+fn nullable_arithmetic(expr: &A, text: &str, message: &str, doc: &Document) -> Option<String> {
+    let A::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = expr else { return None; };
+    if !matches!(op.as_str(), "+" | "-" | "*") || !stable(left) || !stable(right) { return None; }
+    let (a, b) = message.split_once("got ")?.1.split_once(" and ")?;
+    let mut guards = Vec::new();
+    for (operand, ty) in [(left, a), (right, b)] {
+        if ty.ends_with(" ?") { guards.push(format!("{} != null", doc.source.expression(operand))); }
+    }
+    if guards.is_empty() { return None; }
+    // Null propagation retains absence. Choosing zero/now could admit a write
+    // that should refuse, so a required destination receives advice only.
+    Some(format!("({text} if {} else null)", guards.join(" and ")))
+}
+fn insert(expr: &A, text: &str, program: &ProgramDecl, message: &str) -> Option<String> {
+    let A::Write { op: WriteOp::Insert, condition: None, .. } = expr else { return None; };
+    let missing = message.split_once("omits required column")?.1.trim_start_matches('s').trim();
+    let names: Vec<_> = missing.split(", ").collect();
+    if names.iter().any(|name| !program.args.iter().any(|arg| arg.name == *name)) { return None; }
+    // Last brace is the insert payload (condition is excluded above). Preserve
+    // expressions, strings and comments verbatim; refuse commented tails.
+    let end = text.rfind('}')?;
+    let before = text[..end].trim_end();
+    if before.lines().last()?.contains("--") { return None; }
+    let comma = if before.ends_with(['{', ',']) { "" } else { "," };
+    Some(format!("{before}{comma} {} {}", names.join(", "), &text[end..]))
+}
+
+// Only a positive comparison/arithmetic condition. Do not move null guards
+// across not/or, calls, references, or unrelated branches.
+fn positive_contains(condition: &A, site: &Site) -> bool {
+    if condition.site() == site { return true; }
+    match condition {
+        A::Binary {op, left, right, ..} if matches!(op.as_str(), "+"|"-"|"*"|"<"|"<="|">"|">="|"="|"!="|"and") => positive_contains(left, site) || positive_contains(right, site),
+        _ => false,
+    }
+}
+fn guarded_condition<'a>(body: &'a [Stmt], expr: &A, message: &str, doc: &Document) -> Option<(&'a A, Vec<String>)> {
+    let A::Binary {left, right, ..} = expr else { return None; };
+    let (a,b) = message.split_once("got ")?.1.split_once(" and ")?;
+    let mut guards = Vec::new();
+    for (value, ty) in [(left, a), (right,b)] {
+        if ty.ends_with(" ?") { if !stable(value) { return None; } guards.push(format!("{} != null", doc.source.expression(value))); }
+    }
+    if guards.is_empty() { return None; }
+    for statement in body {
+        match statement {
+            Stmt::Require {condition,..} | Stmt::If {condition,..} if positive_contains(condition, expr.site()) => return Some((condition,guards)),
+            Stmt::If {then_body,else_body,..} => if let Some(found) = guarded_condition(then_body,expr,message,doc).or_else(||guarded_condition(else_body,expr,message,doc)) {return Some(found);},
+            _ => {},
+        }
+    }
+    None
+}
+
+#[cfg(test)]
+mod tests {
+    #[test]
+    fn nullable_unique_population_is_never_repaired_with_first_one() {
+        let source = "table tags:\n  slug: text <=40 ?\n  unique bySlug: slug\n  public\nquery q():\n  return tags[slug = null] by .slug\n";
+        let bound = crate::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);
+        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_QUARRY_BOUND"), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
+        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().all(|d| !d.mechanical), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
+    }
+    #[test]
+    fn nullable_guard_has_an_exact_source_span() {
+        let source = "query q(at: time ?):\n  require at + 1 > now else LATE\n  return 1\n";
+        let (doc, _) = crate::parse_document("schema.q", source);
+        let bound = crate::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);
+        assert!(bound.diagnostics.iter().all(|d| d.mechanical), "{:?}\n{:?}", bound.diagnostics, doc.source);
+    }
+}
+
+fn expr_at<'a>(expr: &'a A, site: &Site) -> Option<&'a A> {
+    if expr.site() == site { return Some(expr); }
+    match expr {
+        A::Binary {left,right,..} => expr_at(left,site).or_else(||expr_at(right,site)),
+        A::Unary {value,..} => expr_at(value,site),
+        A::Member {base,..} => expr_at(base,site),
+        _ => None,
+    }
+}
+
+fn total_unique(expr: &A, documents: &[Document]) -> bool {
+    let A::Read(read) = expr else { return false; };
+    let Some(table) = documents.iter().flat_map(|doc| &doc.tables).find(|table| table.name == read.table) else { return false; };
+    let named = |column: &str| read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.as_deref() == Some(column));
+    if named("id") { return true; }
+    table.indexes.iter().any(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.columns.len() == read.keys.len()
+        && index.columns.iter().all(|column| (column == "id" || table.columns.iter().any(|c| c.name == *column && !c.type_.optional))
+            && (named(column) || read.index.as_ref() == Some(&index.name) && read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_none()))))
+}

+fn show_diff(name: &str, before: &str, after: &str) {
+    let (old, new): (Vec<_>, Vec<_>) = (before.lines().collect(), after.lines().collect());
+    let prefix = old.iter().zip(&new).take_while(|(a, b)| a == b).count();
+    let suffix = old[prefix..].iter().rev().zip(new[prefix..].iter().rev()).take_while(|(a, b)| a == b).count();
+    eprintln!("--- {name}\n+++ {name}\n@@ line {} @@", prefix + 1);
+    for line in &old[prefix..old.len() - suffix] { eprintln!("-{line}"); }
+    for line in &new[prefix..new.len() - suffix] { eprintln!("+{line}"); }
+}

 fn tokens(text: &str) -> Vec<Token> {

 }

+#[test]
+fn wave34_nullable_bool_time_missing_insert_and_parameter_case_resolve_in_one_fix_run() {
+    // Wave 34 E_QUARRY_TYPE/UNKNOWN: nullable times and bools, and required
+    // insert columns already present as arguments. No default values invented.
+    for (bad, good) in [
+        ("query q(flag: bool ?):\n  if flag:\n    return 1\n  return 0\n", "query q(flag: bool ?):\n  if (flag) = true:\n    return 1\n  return 0\n"),
+        ("query q(flag: bool ?):\n  require flag else FLAG\n  return 1\n", "query q(flag: bool ?):\n  require (flag) = true else FLAG\n  return 1\n"),
+        ("query q(at: time ?):\n  return at + 1 -- café\n", "query q(at: time ?):\n  return (at + 1 if at != null else null) -- café\n"),
+        ("query q(at: time ?):\n  require at + 1 > now else LATE\n  return 1\n", "query q(at: time ?):\n  require at != null and (at + 1 > now) else LATE\n  return 1\n"),
+        ("query q(Title: text <=20):\n  return title\n", "query q(Title: text <=20):\n  return Title\n"),
+        ("table notes:\n  title: text <=20\n  public\nmutation add(title: text <=20):\n  return insert notes {}\n", "table notes:\n  title: text <=20\n  public\nmutation add(title: text <=20):\n  return insert notes { title }\n"),

+        let again = check_fix(&[("schema.q", good, good)], true);
+        assert!(!String::from_utf8_lossy(&again.stderr).contains("fix:"));
+    }
+}
+
+#[test]

import test from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import { mkdtempSync, mkdirSync, writeFileSync, rmSync } from 'node:fs';
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { spawnSync } from 'node:child_process';
import { proofFailure, startDev } from '../dist/proof.js';
import { proofRole, observeProofReply } from '../dist/proof-observation.js';
import { createClient } from '../dist/client.js';
import { binary } from './helpers/app-server.mjs';

function directory(t) {
 const dir=mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(),'calls1-proof-'));
 t.after(()=>rmSync(dir,{recursive:true,force:true})); mkdirSync(join(dir,'snapback'));
 writeFileSync(join(dir,'snapback/schema.q'),`use personas customer, staff
query amount(n: int):
  return n
`); return dir;
}
// Wave 34 Muse ERR_ASSERTION/E_INPUT/E_ASSET_UNKNOWN/E_AUTH/E_ADMISSION:
// report refusal content without exposing request arguments or credentials.
for(const code of ['E_INPUT','E_ASSET_UNKNOWN','E_AUTH','E_ADMISSION']) test(`wave34 Muse assertion: ${code} leads with operation, role, field and fix`,()=>{
 proofRole('PRIVATE-TOKEN','staff');
 observeProofReply('http://127.0.0.1:4400/m/approve',{authorization:'Bearer PRIVATE-TOKEN'}, {why:{code,site:'args.evidence',message:'PRIVATE-ROW'}});
 const error=new assert.AssertionError({actual:{state:'failed',why:{code}},expected:{state:'sent'},operator:'deepStrictEqual'});
 const original={stack:error.stack,message:error.message,actual:error.actual};
 const summary=proofFailure(error);
 assert.match(summary,new RegExp(`operation approve; role staff; ${code}; field args.evidence`));
 assert.match(summary,/likely fix:/); assert.doesNotMatch(summary,/PRIVATE-/);
 assert.equal(error.stack,original.stack);assert.equal(error.message,original.message);assert.equal(error.actual,original.actual);
});

test('wave34 acceptance uncoded red: real E_INPUT request remains visible before the native assertion diff', t=>{
 const cwd=directory(t), proof=new URL('../dist/proof.js',import.meta.url).href, client=new URL('../dist/client.js',import.meta.url).href;
 const source=`import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import {startDev} from ${JSON.stringify(proof)};
import {createClient} from ${JSON.stringify(client)};
const host=await startDev({cwd:${JSON.stringify(cwd)},env:{...process.env,SNAPBACK4_BIN:${JSON.stringify(binary)}},args:['--memory','--no-watch']});
const c=createClient({url:host.url,persona:'customer'});
try { const result=await c.query({name:'amount'},{n:null}); assert.equal(result.data,7); }
finally {c.close();await host.stop();}
`;
 const file=join(cwd,'red.mjs');writeFileSync(file,source);
 const env={...process.env};delete env.NODE_TEST_CONTEXT;
 const result=spawnSync(process.execPath,[file],{cwd,env,encoding:'utf8',timeout:30000});
 assert.equal(result.status,1,result.stdout+result.stderr);
 const output=result.stderr;
 assert.match(output,/proof refusal \(nearest observed request\): operation amount; role customer; E_INPUT/);
 assert.ok(output.indexOf('proof refusal')<output.indexOf('AssertionError'),output);
 assert.match(output,/likely fix:/);assert.match(output,/AssertionError/);
});

test('wave34 nearest-event summary identifies an observed request without claiming it caused the assertion',async t=>{
 const cwd=directory(t), host=await startDev({cwd,env:{...process.env,SNAPBACK4_BIN:binary},args:['--memory','--no-watch']});
 const c=createClient({url:host.url,persona:'customer'});
 t.after(async()=>{c.close();await host.stop();});
 const read=await c.query({name:'amount'},{n:null});assert.equal(read.denied.code,'E_INPUT');
 const error=new assert.AssertionError({actual:0,expected:1,operator:'strictEqual'});
 assert.match(proofFailure(error),/nearest observed request.*operation amount; role customer; E_INPUT/);
});

test('matching codes alone do not establish the operation or field that failed',()=>{
 const error = new assert.AssertionError({actual:{why:{code:'E_INPUT'}},expected:1,operator:'strictEqual'});
 const summary=proofFailure(error,[{operation:'unrelated',role:'other',code:'E_INPUT',field:'other.field'}]);
 assert.match(summary,/nearest observed request/);
});

test('device admission refusal keeps its operation and persona before a state-only assertion', async t=>{
 const {createLocalClient}=await import('../dist/local.js');
 const {processDevice}=await import('../dist/native.js');
 const cwd=directory(t);
 writeFileSync(join(cwd,'snapback/schema.q'),`use personas customer
 tablePlaceholder`.replace(' tablePlaceholder',`table notes:
  owner: principal
  n: int
  by byOwner: owner, id
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  sync to .owner
mutation put(n: int):
  require n > 0 else POSITIVE
  return insert notes {owner: viewer, n}
query list():
  return notes[viewer] first 10 by byOwner
`));
 const host=await startDev({cwd,env:{...process.env,SNAPBACK4_BIN:binary},args:['--memory','--no-watch']});
 const c=await createLocalClient({url:host.url,persona:'customer',device:processDevice({binary,dir:join(cwd,'device')})});
 t.after(async()=>{await c.close();await host.stop();});
 await c.sync();
 const write=await c.mutate({name:'put'},{n:-1});
 assert.equal(write.state,'failed',JSON.stringify(write));assert.equal(write.why.code,'POSITIVE');
 const error=new assert.AssertionError({actual:write,expected:{state:'sent'},operator:'deepStrictEqual'});
 const summary=proofFailure(error);
 assert.match(summary,/operation put; role customer; POSITIVE/);
 assert.doesNotMatch(summary,/nearest observed request/,'the actual refusal object carries request correlation');
});

test('wave34 Codex 003/004/007: init owns the frontend install and preserves npm failure without polling', {skip:process.platform==='win32'},async t=>{
 const {chmodSync}=await import('node:fs');
 const cwd=mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(),'calls1-init-'));t.after(()=>rmSync(cwd,{recursive:true,force:true}));
 const bin=join(cwd,'bin');mkdirSync(bin);
 writeFileSync(join(bin,'npm'),'#!/bin/sh\necho PRIVATE_NPM_PROGRESS\nsleep 0.2\nexit 17\n');chmodSync(join(bin,'npm'),0o755);
 // Defer schema-specific proofs but exercise the real generated installer.
 const {cli}=await import('../dist/proof.js');
 const result=await cli(['init','--web','--online','--password'],{cwd,env:{...process.env,PATH:`${bin}:${process.env.PATH}`,SNAPBACK4_BIN:binary,SNAPBACK4_INIT_INSTALL:'1'}});
 assert.equal(result.code,17,result.stderr+result.stdout);
 assert.match(result.stdout,/installing frontend dependencies/);
 assert.doesNotMatch(result.stdout+result.stderr,/PRIVATE_NPM_PROGRESS|still running/);
 const {readFileSync}=await import('node:fs');
 const state=JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(cwd,'.snapback4/install.json'),'utf8'));
 assert.equal(state.state,'finished');assert.equal(state.code,17);
 assert.match(readFileSync(join(cwd,'.snapback4/install.log'),'utf8'),/PRIVATE_NPM_PROGRESS/);
});

diff --git a/snapback4/packages/snapback4/CHANGELOG.md b/snapback4/packages/snapback4/CHANGELOG.md
index 511eb4693b..7280e401ff 100644
--- a/snapback4/packages/snapback4/CHANGELOG.md
+++ b/snapback4/packages/snapback4/CHANGELOG.md
@@ -4,6 +4,8 @@
 
 ### Guide changes
 
+- Image-only backends accept client.upload file metadata through the verified image path after a file-kind refusal; invalid bytes and image bounds still refuse. File scenarios are labelled inapplicable when the schema has no file column. Frontend install logs stay in .snapback4/install.log; init owns one blocking install with bounded start/completion output, preserving its exit status. Explicit install joins print a single completion line.
+
 - Start replaces the introductory card tour with one compiling customer/staff workflow and feature pointers. Default cards have a hard reading budget; project repeats show deltas (guide --repeat/--full restore content). Build-time API digests and a generated operation index replace declaration greps. Init shows the recipe and file map before a background frontend install with status/wait. Probe scripts announce their lifetime, refuse repeated empty long runs and bound idle foreground waiting unless the npm script receives --hold; probe:check verifies. Upload placement explains separate uploads for several image columns.
 
 - Production proofs have one serve/accounts/static-host/SIGTERM recipe. Roster mismatches name supplied and pinned plans. Inherited serve drivers get a serve-check --then next step on upgrade; the proof preserves the requested uptime. waitForFresh can observe a stable control before its trigger. Admin data --all and snapshotAll inspect all active rows; generated query help exposes next. Reads show the complete-empty absent arm; ambiguous signup advises login without revealing account existence.
diff --git a/snapback4/packages/snapback4/test/calls1-uploads.test.mjs b/snapback4/packages/snapback4/test/calls1-uploads.test.mjs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..afd15dae44
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/packages/snapback4/test/calls1-uploads.test.mjs
@@ -0,0 +1,31 @@
+import test from 'node:test';
+import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
+import { createClient } from '../dist/client.js';
+import { server } from './helpers/app-server.mjs';
+
+const png = Buffer.from('iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAAEAAAABCAQAAAC1HAwCAAAAC0lEQVR42mP8/x8AAwMCAO+/b1sAAAAASUVORK5CYII=', 'base64');
+// Wave 34 E_ASSET_TYPE: Codex/Muse builders pass file metadata for an image-only backend.
+test('wave34 image-only upload accepts file MIME/name/size metadata but verifies bytes and bounds', async t => {
+  const f = await server(t, '', `use personas alice
+table photos:
+  photo: image <=1000
+  public
+`);
+  const client = createClient({ url: f.url, persona: 'alice' });
+  t.after(() => client.close());
+  const options = { file: { type: 'image/png', name: 'photo.png', size: png.length } };
+  const image = await client.upload(png, options);
+  assert.equal(image.ok, true, JSON.stringify(image));
+  assert.equal(image.asset.type, 'image/png');
+  assert.equal(image.asset.bytes, png.length);
+  assert.equal(image.asset.width, 1);
+  const fake = await client.upload(Buffer.from('this is not a png'), options);
+  assert.equal(fake.ok, false);
+  assert.equal(fake.why.code, 'E_ASSET_TYPE');
+  const large = await client.upload(Buffer.concat([png, Buffer.alloc(1000)]), options);
+  assert.equal(large.ok, false);
+  assert.equal(large.why.code, 'E_ASSET_TOO_LARGE');
+  const pdf = await client.upload(Buffer.from('%PDF-1.7\n'), { file: { type: 'application/pdf', name: 'x.pdf' } });
+  assert.equal(pdf.ok, false);
+  assert.equal(pdf.why.code, 'E_ASSET_TYPE');
+});
