//! Verify executable repairs in the affected declaration's context.
//! Retain caller operands; never recursively verify a candidate's diagnostics.
use crate::{ast::*, bind, parser, Bound, Diagnostic, Site};
use std::ops::Range;

mod safe;

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub(crate) enum ReadChange { NamedKey { columns: Vec<String>, index: Option<String> }, Index(String), Point, SearchOrder { keys: Vec<String>, order: Vec<String> } }

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub(crate) struct ReadRepair {
    pub site: Site,
    pub changes: Vec<ReadChange>,
}

// @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — index advice is source only after the patched
// declaration passes the real parser, planner and admission in its own scope.
pub(crate) fn verify_bound(documents: &[Document], diagnostics: &mut [Diagnostic]) {
    for diagnostic in diagnostics.iter_mut() {
        let Some(repair) = diagnostic.read_repair.take() else { continue; };
        let verify = |change: &ReadChange| {
            let candidate = read_candidate(documents, &repair.site, change)?;
            (check_context(documents, &candidate, true) == Check::Accepted).then_some(candidate)
        };
        let named = repair.changes.iter().filter(|change| matches!(change, ReadChange::NamedKey { .. }))
            .filter_map(verify).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        if !named.is_empty() {
            diagnostic.message = format!("{}; choose a named form: {}", diagnostic.message.split(';').next().unwrap(), named.iter().map(|candidate| candidate.rewrite.as_str()).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" or "));
        }
        let selected = named.into_iter().next().or_else(|| repair.changes.iter()
            .filter(|change| !matches!(change, ReadChange::NamedKey { .. })).find_map(verify));
        if let Some(candidate) = selected {
            if diagnostic.code == "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN" {
                let before = &candidate.source[..candidate.replacement.start];
                let line = candidate.declaration_site.line + before.bytes().filter(|b| *b == b'\n').count() as u32;
                let col = before.rsplit('\n').next().unwrap().chars().count() as u32 + 1;
                diagnostic.verified_edit = Some((Site::new(&candidate.site.file, line, col), candidate.source[candidate.replacement].into()));
            }
            diagnostic.rewrite = candidate.rewrite;
        } else { diagnostic.rewrite.clear(); }
        if diagnostic.rewrite.is_empty() { diagnostic.mechanical = false; }
    }
    safe::verify(documents, diagnostics);
}

fn read_candidate(documents: &[Document], site: &Site, change: &ReadChange) -> Option<Candidate> {
    let doc = documents.iter().find(|doc| doc.file == site.file)?;
    let (read, text, grouped, start) = doc.source.read(site)?;
    let mut fixed = read.clone();
    match change {
        ReadChange::NamedKey { columns, index } => {
            if fixed.keys.len() != columns.len() { return None; }
            for (key, column) in fixed.keys.iter_mut().zip(columns) { key.column = Some(column.clone()); }
            fixed.index = index.clone();
        }
        ReadChange::Index(index) => { fixed.index = Some(index.clone()); }
        ReadChange::SearchOrder { keys, order } => {
            if fixed.matching.is_none() || fixed.keys.len() > keys.len() { return None; }
            for (key, column) in fixed.keys.iter_mut().zip(keys) { key.column.get_or_insert_with(|| column.clone()); }
            fixed.index = None;
            fixed.order_keys = order.clone();
        }
        ReadChange::Point => {
            // A point cannot silently discard a population predicate or cursor.
            if read.filter.is_some() || read.matching.is_some() || !read.seeks.is_empty()
                || !read.order_keys.is_empty() || read.range.as_ref()?.cursor.is_some() { return None; }
            fixed.range = None;
        }
    }
    // Retain the caller's operands, including grouping, literals and comments.
    // Never invent expressions from the selected index's column names.
    let fixed = Expr::Read(fixed);
    let rewrite = crate::print::with_source_at(&fixed, if grouped { 8 } else { 0 }, &|expr| doc.source.get(expr));
    let (declaration_site, source, replacement) = doc.source.declarations.iter().find_map(|(key, source)| {
        let (key, col) = key.rsplit_once(':')?;
        let (file, line) = key.rsplit_once(':')?;
        let declaration_site = Site::new(file, line.parse().ok()?, col.parse().ok()?);
        if file != site.file { return None; }
        let line = start.line.checked_sub(declaration_site.line)? as usize;
        let lines = source.split('\n').collect::<Vec<_>>();
        let column = start.col.checked_sub(1)? as usize;
        let byte = lines.get(line)?.char_indices().map(|(i, _)| i).chain([lines[line].len()]).nth(column)?;
        let offset = lines[..line].iter().map(|s| s.len() + 1).sum::<usize>() + byte;
        let span = offset..offset.checked_add(text.len())?;
        (source.get(span.clone())? == text.as_str()).then_some((declaration_site, source.clone(), span))
    })?;
    Some(Candidate { site: site.clone(), declaration_site, source, replacement, rewrite, intended: None, message: None })
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub(crate) struct Candidate {
    pub site: Site,
    pub declaration_site: Site,
    pub source: String,
    pub replacement: Range<usize>,
    pub rewrite: String,
    pub intended: Option<ReadExpr>,
    pub message: Option<String>,
}

pub(crate) fn verify(documents: &[Document], diagnostics: &mut [Diagnostic]) {
    for diagnostic in diagnostics {
        // Other diagnostics include design advice rather than executable edits.
        // These are the concrete repairs introduced by the familiarity lane.
        if diagnostic.code != "E_QUARRY_SELF_EQUALITY"
            && !(diagnostic.code == "E_QUARRY_UNSUPPORTED" && diagnostic.message.starts_with("count(*)")) { continue; }
        if diagnostic.rewrite.is_empty() { continue; }
        let candidate = documents.iter().flat_map(|doc| &doc.source.repairs).rev()
            .find(|candidate| candidate.site == diagnostic.site && candidate.rewrite == diagnostic.rewrite);
        let verified = candidate.and_then(|candidate| {
            // A where population cannot become a nullable unique point, even
            // when the bare point compiles (for example, a required outer key).
            if nullable_key(documents, candidate) && let Some(page) = key_page(documents, candidate) {
                return (check(documents, &page) == Check::Accepted).then_some(page);
            }
            match check(documents, candidate) {
                Check::Accepted => Some(candidate.clone()),
                Check::Unbound => {
                    let page = key_page(documents, candidate)?;
                    (check(documents, &page) == Check::Accepted).then_some(page)
                }
                Check::Rejected => None,
            }
        });
        if let Some(candidate) = verified {
            diagnostic.rewrite = candidate.rewrite;
            if let Some(message) = candidate.message { diagnostic.message = message; }
        } else { diagnostic.rewrite.clear(); }
    }
}

#[derive(PartialEq, Eq)]
enum Check { Accepted, Unbound, Rejected }

fn nullable_key(documents: &[Document], candidate: &Candidate) -> bool {
    let Some(read) = &candidate.intended else { return false; };
    let Some(table) = documents.iter().flat_map(|doc| &doc.tables).find(|table| table.name == read.table) else { return false; };
    table.indexes.iter().any(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain()
        && index.columns.len() == read.keys.len()
        && index.columns.iter().all(|column| read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column)))
        && index.columns.iter().any(|column| table.columns.iter().any(|c| &c.name == column && c.type_.optional)))
}

// A nullable key must retain a population. Other page fallbacks retry only
// after a binding failure, never to bypass a compiling wrong plan.
fn key_page(documents: &[Document], candidate: &Candidate) -> Option<Candidate> {
    let mut page = candidate.clone();
    let read = page.intended.as_mut()?;
    if read.range.is_some() || read.filter.is_some() || read.matching.is_some()
        || !read.seeks.is_empty() || !read.order_keys.is_empty() { return None; }
    let table = documents.iter().flat_map(|doc| &doc.tables).find(|table| table.name == read.table)?;
    let pinned = |column: &str| read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.as_deref() == Some(column));
    // first 1 must preserve the entire population. Nullable unique indexes
    // can contain tied nulls and do not establish a one-row bound.
    let total = pinned("id") || table.indexes.iter().any(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain()
        && index.columns.iter().all(|column| pinned(column) && (column == "id"
            || table.columns.iter().any(|c| &c.name == column && !c.type_.optional))));
    if nullable_key(documents, candidate) {
        // @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — preserve every nullable unique tie with
        // an explicit population order; verification still checks its bound.
        read.order_keys.push("id".into());
        page.rewrite.push_str(" by .id");
        if let Some(message) = &mut page.message { message.push_str(" with `by .id` to keep every null match"); }
    } else if total {
        read.range = Some(RangeSpec { order: Order::Asc, take: 1, cursor: None, site: read.site.clone() });
        page.rewrite.push_str(" first 1");
        if let Some(message) = &mut page.message { message.push_str(" with `first 1` to keep a page"); }
    } else { return None; }
    Some(page)
}

fn check(documents: &[Document], candidate: &Candidate) -> Check {
    check_context(documents, candidate, false)
}

fn check_context(documents: &[Document], candidate: &Candidate, keep_operations: bool) -> Check {
    let Some(patched) = parser::repairs::splice(&candidate.source,
        vec![(candidate.replacement.clone(), candidate.rewrite.clone())]) else { return Check::Rejected; };
    // Retain original source sites without reparsing any other declaration.
    let source = format!("{}{patched}", "\n".repeat(candidate.declaration_site.line.saturating_sub(1) as usize));
    let tables = documents.iter().flat_map(|doc| doc.tables.iter().cloned()).collect();
    let (replacement, diagnostics) = parser::parse_with_types(&candidate.site.file, &source, tables, Default::default());
    if !diagnostics.is_empty() { return Check::Rejected; }
    // Index diagnostics arise while binding a rule or an actual helper/shape
    // expansion. Retaining all operations retains those callers and their
    // focal types. Declaration-local parser repairs have no such proof for
    // a standalone shape, or for a changed helper signature.
    if !keep_operations && !replacement.shapes.is_empty() { return Check::Rejected; }
    for function in &replacement.functions {
        if documents.iter().flat_map(|doc| &doc.functions).any(|old|
            affected(candidate, &old.site) && old.args.len() != function.args.len()) { return Check::Rejected; }
    }
    let mut context = context(documents, candidate, replacement, keep_operations);
    // With callers retained, their real mode and placement are the proof; an
    // extra query would incorrectly reject helpers that write in a mutation.
    // The declaration-local path has no retained caller, so probe that helper
    // with its typed arguments instead of silently leaving its body unchecked.
    for doc in context.iter_mut().filter(|_| !keep_operations) {
        if let Some(function) = doc.functions.iter().find(|decl| affected(candidate, &decl.site)) {
            let Some(probe) = helper_probe(&doc.file, function) else { return Check::Rejected; };
            doc.programs.push(probe);
        }
    }
    let actual = bind::bind_unverified(context.clone());

//! Authoring-only source spans. Never part of the schema, IR or device payload.
use crate::{ast::Expr, lexer::{Kind, Token}};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct SourceMap {
    pub(crate) expressions: BTreeMap<String, (Expr, String, bool, crate::Site)>,
    pub(crate) declarations: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    pub(crate) repairs: Vec<crate::repair::Candidate>,
}

impl SourceMap {
    pub(crate) fn extend(&mut self, other: &Self) {
        self.expressions.extend(other.expressions.clone());
        self.declarations.extend(other.declarations.clone());
    }

    pub(crate) fn record(&mut self, expr: &Expr, lines: &[String], tokens: &[Token]) {
        // Block expressions consume layout tokens after their final source
        // token. A dedent's site can already be on the next declaration.
        let Some(end) = tokens.iter().rposition(|t| !matches!(t.kind, Kind::Newline | Kind::Indent | Kind::Dedent | Kind::Eof)) else { return };
        let tokens = &tokens[..=end];
        let Some(first) = tokens.first() else { return };
        let Some(last) = tokens.last() else { return };
        let Some(line) = lines.get(last.site.line as usize - 1) else { return };
        let tail: Vec<_> = line.chars().skip(last.site.col as usize - 1).collect();
        let mut length = last.value.chars().count();
        if last.kind == Kind::Str {
            length = 1;
            while length < tail.len() {
                let c = tail[length];
                length += 1;
                if c == tail[0] { break; }
                if c == '\\' { length += 1; }
            }
        }
        let mut text = String::new();
        for number in first.site.line..=last.site.line {
            let Some(line) = lines.get(number as usize - 1) else { return };
            if number != first.site.line { text.push('\n'); }
            let start = if number == first.site.line { first.site.col as usize - 1 } else { 0 };
            let end = if number == last.site.line { last.site.col as usize - 1 + length } else { line.chars().count() };
            text.extend(line.chars().skip(start).take(end.saturating_sub(start)));
        }
        let mut depth = 0_i32;
        let grouped = first.kind == Kind::Symbol && first.value == "(" && tokens.iter().enumerate().all(|(i, t)| {
            if t.kind == Kind::Symbol {
                if t.value == "(" { depth += 1; }
                if t.value == ")" { depth -= 1; }
            }
            depth > 0 || i + 1 == tokens.len()
        }) && depth == 0;
        self.expressions.insert(expr.site().to_string(), (expr.clone(), text, grouped, first.site.clone()));
    }

    pub(crate) fn get(&self, expr: &Expr) -> Option<(String, bool)> {
        let (syntax, text, grouped, _) = self.expressions.get(&expr.site().to_string())?;
        // A planner may reuse a site while changing the expression.
        (syntax == expr).then(|| (text.clone(), *grouped))
    }

    pub(crate) fn read(&self, site: &crate::Site) -> Option<(crate::ast::ReadExpr, String, bool, crate::Site)> {
        let (expr, text, grouped, start) = self.expressions.get(&site.to_string())?;
        if let Expr::Read(read) = expr { return Some((read.clone(), text.clone(), *grouped, start.clone())); }
        // Keyed write diagnostics share the read repair verifier. Replace only
        // the target; keep the write kind, condition and payload in its caller.
        let Expr::Write { table, keys, site, .. } = expr else { return None; };
        let tokens = crate::lexer::lex("", text).0;
        let table_token = tokens.get(1)?;
        let mut depth = 0;
        let close = tokens.iter().skip(2).find(|token| {
            if token.kind != Kind::Symbol { return false; }
            match token.value.as_str() { "[" => depth += 1, "]" => { depth -= 1; if depth == 0 { return true; } }, _ => {} }
            false
        })?;
        let offset = |site: &crate::Site| {
            let lines = text.split_inclusive('\n').collect::<Vec<_>>();
            let line = site.line.checked_sub(1)? as usize;
            Some(lines.get(..line)?.iter().map(|s| s.len()).sum::<usize>() + lines.get(line)?.char_indices()
                .map(|(i, _)| i).nth(site.col.checked_sub(1)? as usize)?)
        };
        let from = offset(&table_token.site)?;
        let end = offset(&close.site)? + 1;
        let mut target_site = start.clone();
        target_site.line += table_token.site.line - 1;
        target_site.col = if table_token.site.line == 1 { start.col + table_token.site.col - 1 } else { table_token.site.col };
        Some((crate::ast::ReadExpr { table: table.clone(), keys: keys.clone(), site: site.clone(), index: None,
            cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), order_keys: Vec::new(), range: None, matching: None, filter: None, seeks: Vec::new() },
            text[from..end].into(), false, target_site))
    }

    pub(crate) fn expression(&self, expr: &Expr) -> String {
        crate::print::with_source(expr, &|expr| self.get(expr))
    }

    /// A comparison's operand needs parentheses around booleans and conditionals.
    pub(crate) fn operand(&self, expr: &Expr) -> String {
        crate::print::with_source_at(expr, 5, &|expr| self.get(expr))
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    #[test]
    fn explicit_index_search_sites_retain_authored_spelling() {
        let bound = crate::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), "table posts:\n  body: text <=40\n  at: time\n  by byWords: words(body), at, id\n  public\n  sync public\nquery posts(q: text <=40):\n  return posts matching q first 10 by byWords\n".into())]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        let sites = &bound.schema.operations["posts"].sites;
        assert_eq!(sites.len(), 2, "search and browse alternatives");
        assert!(sites.iter().all(|site| site.site == "schema.q:8:10 (posts matching q first 10 by byWords)"), "{sites:?}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn canonical_backend_sites_do_not_replace_authored_diagnostic_spelling() {
        let bound = crate::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), "table posts:\n  public\n  sync public\nquery posts():\n  return posts first 10\n".into())]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        let program = &bound.programs[0];
        assert_eq!(program.sources["programs.posts.0"].1, "posts first 10");
        assert_eq!(bound.schema.operations["posts"].sites[0].site, "schema.q:5:10 (posts[] first 10)");
    }
}

// API signatures: snapback4 guide api proof | client | auth | generated
// A private worker group owns npm; IPC EOF stops it even if this owner is killed.
import { spawn } from "node:child_process";
import { mkdirSync, readFileSync, writeFileSync, openSync, closeSync, unlinkSync, chmodSync } from "node:fs";
import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
const args = process.argv.slice(2);
if (args[0] === "--worker") {
  if (!process.send || !process.connected) throw Error("install worker requires its owning IPC channel");
  const log = openSync(".snapback4/install.log", "a", 0o600);
  const npm = spawn("npm", ["install", "--no-fund", "--no-audit", ...args.slice(1)], { stdio: ["ignore", log, log] });
  closeSync(log);
  const stop = () => {
    if (process.platform === "win32") { npm.kill("SIGKILL"); process.exit(1); }
    // The owner spawned THIS process as its private group leader. Never read a
    // persisted PID/group to authorize a signal.
    process.kill(-process.pid, "SIGKILL");
  };
  process.once("disconnect", stop); process.once("message", stop);
  npm.once("error", error => { console.error(`npm install could not start: ${error.message}`); process.send?.({code:1}); });
  npm.once("exit", (code, signal) => { process.send?.({code:code ?? 1, signal}); });
  // Remain group leader until the owner closes IPC, reclaiming descendants too.
} else {
  mkdirSync(".snapback4", { recursive: true, mode: 0o700 });
  if (process.platform !== "win32") chmodSync(".snapback4", 0o700);
  const requestFile = ".snapback4/install-request.json";
  const requested = () => { try { return JSON.parse(readFileSync(requestFile,"utf8")).id; } catch { return undefined; } };
  const point = id => writeFileSync(requestFile, JSON.stringify({id}), {mode:0o600});
  const stateFile = ".snapback4/install.json", lockFile = ".snapback4/install.lock";
  const read = () => { try { return JSON.parse(readFileSync(stateFile, "utf8")); } catch { return null; } };
  const alive = pid => { try { process.kill(pid, 0); return true; } catch (error) { return error.code === "EPERM"; } };
  const report = s => console.log(s ? `frontend install: ${s.state}; ${Math.round((Date.now() - s.started) / 1000)}s elapsed${s.code === undefined ? "" : `; exit ${s.code}`}; log .snapback4/install.log` : "frontend install: not started; node scripts/install.mjs");
  const wait = async () => {
    const pendingSince = Date.now();
    for (;;) {
      const s = read();
      if (requested() && s?.request !== requested()) {
        if (Date.now() - pendingSince > 30_000) { console.error("frontend install: requested owner did not start within 30s; inspect .snapback4/install.log"); return 1; }
        await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, 100)); continue;
      }
      if (!s || s.state !== "running" || !alive(s.pid)) { report(s); return s?.state === "finished" ? s.code : 1; }
      await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, 1000));
    }
  };
  if (args.includes("--status")) { if (requested() && read()?.request !== requested()) console.log("frontend install: requested run starting; earlier receipt is not this run"); else report(read()); }
  else if (args.includes("--wait")) process.exitCode = await wait();
  else {
    const request = process.env.SNAPBACK4_INSTALL_REQUEST ?? randomUUID();
    point(request);
    let locked = false;
    try { const fd = openSync(lockFile, "wx", 0o600); writeFileSync(fd, String(process.pid)); closeSync(fd); locked = true; }
    catch (error) {
      if (error.code !== "EEXIST") throw error;
      let pid; try { pid = Number(readFileSync(lockFile, "utf8")); } catch {}
      if (pid > 0 && alive(pid)) {
        const until = Date.now() + 3000;
        while ((read()?.pid !== pid || !read()?.request) && Date.now() < until) await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 10));
        const running = read();
        if (running?.pid === pid && running.request) point(running.request);
        process.exitCode = args.includes("--background") ? 0 : await wait();
      }
      else { unlinkSync(lockFile); console.error("frontend install: stale owner removed; rerun node scripts/install.mjs"); process.exitCode = 1; }
    }
    if (locked) {
      const started = Date.now(), state = { state: "running", request, pid: process.pid, started };
      const save = extra => writeFileSync(stateFile, JSON.stringify({ ...state, ...extra }) + "\n", { mode: 0o600 });
      save({}); console.error("frontend install: running; read `npx snapback4 guide start` or edit snapback/schema.q meanwhile. Wait once with `node scripts/install.mjs --wait` (allow the command to finish).");
      const child = spawn(process.execPath, [fileURLToPath(import.meta.url), "--worker", ...args.filter(a => a !== "--background")], { detached: process.platform !== "win32", stdio: ["ignore", "inherit", "inherit", "ipc"] });
      let result = 1, done = false;
      const finish = () => {
        if (done) return; done = true;
        save({ state: "finished", code: result }); unlinkSync(lockFile);
        console.error(`npm install finished (exit ${result})`); process.exitCode = result;
      };
      child.once("message", message => { if (Number.isInteger(message?.code)) result = message.code; child.disconnect(); });
      child.once("error", error => { console.error(`npm install could not start: ${error.message}`); finish(); });
      child.once("exit", finish);
      for (const signal of ["SIGTERM", "SIGINT"]) process.once(signal, () => { result = 1; if (child.connected) child.disconnect(); });
    }
  }
}

import { installProofFailureReporting } from "./proof-failure.ts";
// Existing authored proof scripts may import only the client entry.

// fetch promise on macOS (undici#5544). Keep the host Fetch API and pass a
// private fixed dispatcher, including through a host's fetch instrumentation.
import { createRequire } from "node:module";
import { channel } from "node:diagnostics_channel";
import type { Socket } from "node:net";
import type { Dispatcher } from "undici";
const require = createRequire(import.meta.url);
// Import the pinned implementation directly: undici's root import installs a
// process-wide dispatcher as a side effect. SDK requests need no global change.
const Agent: typeof import("undici").Agent = require("undici/lib/dispatcher/agent.js");
const Dispatcher1Wrapper: typeof import("undici").Dispatcher1Wrapper = require("undici/lib/dispatcher/dispatcher1-wrapper.js");
const Client: typeof import("undici").Client = require("undici/lib/dispatcher/client.js");
const Pool: typeof import("undici").Pool = require("undici/lib/dispatcher/pool.js");
// The pinned dispatcher's request owns this handler through queues and socket
// selection. Do not correlate by async context: a pool can drain a different
// caller's request on the completing caller's stack.
type Attempt = { socket?: Socket; read?: number; reused?: boolean; origin: string; path: string; sameTarget?: boolean; dispatches: number };
const attempts = new WeakMap<object, Attempt>();
const requests = new WeakMap<object, Attempt>();
const completed = new WeakSet<Socket>();
let creating: Attempt | undefined;
class TrackedClient extends Client {
  override dispatch(options: Dispatcher.DispatchOptions, handler: Dispatcher.DispatchHandler): boolean {
    const previous = creating;
    creating = attempts.get(handler);
    try { return super.dispatch(options, handler); }
    finally { creating = previous; }
  }
}
type Sent = { request: { origin: string; path: string }; socket: Socket };
channel("undici:request:create").subscribe(message => {
  if (creating) requests.set((message as Sent).request, creating);
});
channel("undici:client:sendHeaders").subscribe(message => {
  const { request, socket } = message as Sent;
  const attempt = requests.get(request);
  if (!attempt) return;
  attempt.socket = socket;
  attempt.read = socket.bytesRead;
  attempt.reused = completed.has(socket);
  attempt.sameTarget = String(request.origin) === attempt.origin && request.path === attempt.path;
});
channel("undici:request:trailers").subscribe(message => {
  const { request } = message as Sent;
  const socket = requests.get(request)?.socket;
  if (socket) completed.add(socket);
});

// @ref LLP 3000#http-overload-diagnostics — retire idle sockets well before the host's five seconds; held responses keep their independent deadlines.
const agent = new Agent({ keepAliveTimeout: 1000, keepAliveMaxTimeout: 1000, keepAliveTimeoutThreshold: 1000,
  factory: (origin, options) => new Pool(origin, { ...options, factory: (url, opts) => new TrackedClient(url, opts) }),
});

function repeatable(url: URL, init?: RequestInit): boolean {
  const method = (init?.method ?? "GET").toUpperCase();
  if (method === "GET") return /^\/(?:schema|probe|changes|write(?:\/[^/]+)?|api\.ts|native\.wasm)?$/.test(url.pathname);
  // Only replay materialized JSON. Request objects and streamed uploads keep
  // their existing single-dispatch behavior, as do one-view GETs and auth.
  if (method !== "POST" || typeof init?.body !== "string") return false;
  if (url.pathname.startsWith("/q/") || url.pathname === "/sync") return true;
  if (!url.pathname.startsWith("/m/")) return false;
  try {
    const body = JSON.parse(init.body);
    return body?.retry_write === true && typeof body.id === "string" && body.id.length > 0;
  } catch { return false; }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.030#14 — the witness must record even a transport-recovered failure; this private, per-call hook carries no remote text.
export const defaultFetch = async (input: Parameters<typeof fetch>[0], init?: RequestInit,
  { onRetry }: { onRetry?: () => void } = {}): Promise<Response> => {
  const url = typeof input === "string" || input instanceof URL ? new URL(input) : new URL(input.url);
  // Fetch snapshots mutable inputs at dispatch. A retry must keep that same
  // URL, write ID, body and authority even if the caller edits its options.
  const target = input instanceof URL ? url.href : input;
  const settings = { ...init };
  const headers = init?.headers === undefined ? undefined : Object.fromEntries(new Headers(init.headers));
  const send = (dispatcher: Dispatcher) => globalThis.fetch(target, {
    ...settings, ...(headers ? { headers: { ...headers } } : {}), dispatcher,
  } as RequestInit);
  const attempt: Attempt = { origin: url.origin, path: url.pathname + url.search, dispatches: 0 };
  const dispatcher = new Dispatcher1Wrapper(agent.compose(dispatch => (options, handler) => {
    attempt.dispatches++;
    attempts.set(handler, attempt);
    // No implicit pipeline replays (GET /assets can consume a view).
    return dispatch({ ...options, idempotent: false }, handler);
  }));
  try {
    return await send(dispatcher);
  } catch (error) {
    const code = (error as { cause?: { code?: string } })?.cause?.code;
    if (input instanceof Request || !repeatable(url, settings) || settings.signal?.aborted
      || attempt.dispatches !== 1 || !attempt.reused || !attempt.sameTarget || !attempt.socket || attempt.socket.bytesRead !== attempt.read
      || !["ECONNRESET", "EPIPE", "UND_ERR_SOCKET"].includes(code ?? "")) throw error;
    // Exactly one fresh-connection attempt. close() drains the response body;
    // destroying at fetch resolution would truncate a streaming /sync reply.
    const retry = new Agent({ pipelining: 0 });
    try {
      onRetry?.();
      return await send(new Dispatcher1Wrapper(retry));
    } finally { void retry.close().catch(() => {}); }
  }
};
// Assets on the client: `upload` posts the bytes and returns the facts the
// server established; `asset` fetches the bytes under the viewer's own
// credentials and hands back a display URL, so a screen never puts a token in
// an <img src> and a one-view photo is never durably cached anywhere
// (LLP 1006 carried into Snapback 4).

import { downloadAsset, assetQueue } from "./asset-download.ts";
import type { AssetProgress } from "./asset-progress.ts";
import type { Asset, AssetRead, Refusal, Uploaded, UploadOptions } from "./contract.ts";
import { dispositionName, uploadFile } from "./files.ts";

export type Bytes = Blob | ArrayBuffer | Uint8Array;

import { refusal, invalidResponse, transportRefusal as offline, retryAfter, jitter } from "./http-response.ts";

// @ref LLP 3000.011.005.000#8 — React Native Blobs use the public FileReader fallback
export function blobArrayBuffer(blob: Blob): Promise<ArrayBuffer> {
  if (typeof blob.arrayBuffer === "function") return blob.arrayBuffer();
  return readBlob(blob, "readAsArrayBuffer");
}

function readBlob(blob: Blob, method: "readAsArrayBuffer"): Promise<ArrayBuffer>;
function readBlob(blob: Blob, method: "readAsDataURL"): Promise<string>;
function readBlob(blob: Blob, method: "readAsArrayBuffer" | "readAsDataURL"): Promise<ArrayBuffer | string> {
  return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    const reader = new FileReader();
    const clear = () => { reader.onload = reader.onerror = reader.onabort = null; };
    reader.onload = () => {
      const result = reader.result;
      clear();
      if (method === "readAsArrayBuffer" && result instanceof ArrayBuffer) resolve(result);
      else if (method === "readAsDataURL" && typeof result === "string") resolve(result);
      else reject(new Error("the Blob reader returned an unexpected result"));
    };
    reader.onerror = () => { const error = reader.error; clear(); reject(error ?? new Error("the Blob read failed")); };
    reader.onabort = () => { clear(); reject(new Error("the Blob read was aborted")); };
    try { reader[method](blob); } catch (error) { clear(); reject(error); }
  });
}

// The types a server serves: media, and the verifiable file types (LLP 3000.038 §2).
const SERVED = ["audio/wav", "audio/mpeg", "audio/flac", "image/jpeg", "image/png", "image/webp", "video/mp4", "application/pdf", "text/plain", "text/csv", "text/markdown",
  "application/json", "image/gif", "application/zip", "application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.wordprocessingml.document",
  "application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.spreadsheetml.sheet", "application/vnd.openxmlformats-officedocument.presentationml.presentation"];

async function blobDataURL(blob: Blob, type: string, bytes: number): Promise<string> {
  if (!SERVED.includes(type)) throw new Error("unsupported asset media type");
  const result = await readBlob(blob, "readAsDataURL"), marker = result.indexOf(";base64,");
  // A text file's Blob may carry `; charset=utf-8` between its type and the encoding.
  const header = marker < 0 ? "" : result.slice(0, marker), encoded = result.slice(marker + ";base64,".length), padding = (3 - bytes % 3) % 3;
  const declared = header.startsWith(`data:${type}`) && (header.length === `data:${type}`.length || /^;\s*charset=utf-8$/i.test(header.slice(`data:${type}`.length)));
  if (!declared || encoded.length !== 4 * Math.ceil(bytes / 3)
    || !/^[A-Za-z0-9+/]*={0,2}$/.test(encoded)
    || (encoded.endsWith("==") ? 2 : encoded.endsWith("=") ? 1 : 0) !== padding) throw new Error("invalid asset data URI");
  return result;
}

// A response Blob belongs to the SDK. Keep it alive until its copy or handle
// is done; native URL revocation alone does not release the host's Blob bytes.
function ownBlob(blob: Blob): () => void {
  let owned: (Blob & { close?: () => void }) | undefined = blob;
  return () => {
    const closing = owned;
    owned = undefined;
    try { closing?.close?.(); } catch { /* Cleanup must not replace the read's result. */ }
  };
}

export async function uploadAsset(base: string, headers: Record<string, string>, doFetch: typeof fetch, bytes: Bytes, canRetry: () => boolean = () => true, options?: UploadOptions, intent?: { id: string; store: string; signal?: AbortSignal | undefined }): Promise<Uploaded> {
  const { "content-type": _json, ...auth } = headers;
  // @ref LLP 3000.038#3 — a file names its kind, filename and declared type; the server checks the type against the bytes.
  const file = options?.file ? uploadFile(bytes, options.file) : undefined;
  let target = file ? `${base}/assets?as=file&name=${encodeURIComponent(file.name)}` : `${base}/assets`;
  if (intent) target += `${file ? "&" : "?"}intent=${encodeURIComponent(intent.id)}&store_id=${encodeURIComponent(intent.store)}`;
  const contentType = file?.type ?? "application/octet-stream";
  let imageFallback = false;
  // Caller-owned buffers may change during admission backoff. Every attempt
  // sends the same selected byte range, including inputs from another realm.
  // Native Blob rejects buffer parts; its fetch accepts an owned buffer view.
  // Blob inputs are already immutable and stay owned by the caller.
  const body = ArrayBuffer.isView(bytes)
    ? new Uint8Array(new Uint8Array(bytes.buffer, bytes.byteOffset, bytes.byteLength))
    : Object.prototype.toString.call(bytes) === "[object ArrayBuffer]"
      ? new Uint8Array(new Uint8Array(bytes as ArrayBuffer)) : bytes as Blob;
  try {
    let waited = 0;
    for (let attempt = 0; ; attempt++) {
      const response = await doFetch(target, { method: "POST", headers: { ...auth, "content-type": contentType }, body, ...(intent?.signal ? { signal: intent.signal } : {}) });
      let json: { asset?: Asset; denied?: Refusal } | null;
      try { json = await response.json(); }
      catch { return { ok: false, why: invalidResponse(response.status, "upload") }; }
      if (response.ok && json?.asset) return { ok: true, asset: json.asset };
      const denied = refusal(json?.denied) ?? invalidResponse(response.status, "upload");
      // @ref LLP 3000.038#3 — metadata does not prove an image's type. A
      // file-less schema refuses before reading/staging, so retry once through
      // its media sniffer, which verifies bytes and the image column's bound.
      if (file && !intent && !imageFallback && denied.code === "E_ASSET_TYPE"
        && denied.message.startsWith("no column of this backend takes a file;")) {
        if (!canRetry()) return { ok: false, why: denied };
        imageFallback = true;
        target = `${base}/assets`;
        continue;
      }
      const why = denied.code === "E_RATE_LIMIT" ? { ...denied, message: `${denied.message}; upload admission is busy: send files sequentially, then retry the same selected bytes after Retry-After (automatic retries are bounded)` } : denied;
      // Only an explicit admission refusal proves these bytes were not stored.
      // A transport failure or another retryable code may follow acceptance.
      if (why.code !== "E_RATE_LIMIT" || why.retryable !== true || attempt === 3 || !canRetry()) return { ok: false, why };

      // Never retry earlier than the server permits. A long Retry-After ends
      // this bounded call instead of parking an upload indefinitely.
      if (waited + delay > 10_000) return { ok: false, why };
      await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, delay));
      waited += delay;
      if (!canRetry()) return { ok: false, why };
    }
  } catch (error) {
    return { ok: false, why: offline(error) };
  }
}

/** Where the bytes are and what to send to get them: for hosts whose image
 * and video views take a URI with headers (React Native). */
export function assetSource(base: string, headers: Record<string, string>, id: string): { uri: string; headers: Record<string, string> } {
  const { "content-type": _json, ...auth } = headers;
  return { uri: `${base}/assets/${encodeURIComponent(id)}`, headers: auth };
}

fn helper_probe(file: &str, function: &FunctionDecl) -> Option<ProgramDecl> {
    let args = function.args.iter().map(|arg| format!("{}: {}", arg.name, crate::print::scalar_type(&arg.type_))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
    let values = function.args.iter().map(|arg| arg.name.as_str()).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
    let source = format!("query repairProbe({args}):\n  return {}({values})\n", function.name);
    let (mut parsed, errors) = parser::parse_document(file, &source);
    if !errors.is_empty() { return None; }
    let mut probe = parsed.programs.pop()?;
    // Like $view: declarations, this compiler-only namespace cannot be
    // authored in Quarry. Neither an operation nor a module can collide.
    probe.name = format!("$repair:{}", function.name);
    Some(probe)
}

fn affected(candidate: &Candidate, site: &Site) -> bool {
    let last = candidate.declaration_site.line + candidate.source.lines().count() as u32;
    site.file == candidate.site.file && (candidate.declaration_site.line..last).contains(&site.line)
}

fn context(documents: &[Document], candidate: &Candidate, replacement: Document, keep_operations: bool) -> Vec<Document> {
    let mut context = documents.to_vec();
    for doc in &mut context {
        // Parser repairs need a declaration-local proof. Index repairs retain
        // every caller as well: switching a range to a point can change a
        // helper's result type or violate a job's chunk/cursor contract.
        if !keep_operations { doc.programs.clear(); doc.actions.clear(); doc.routes.clear(); doc.schedules.clear(); }
        if doc.file != candidate.site.file { continue; }
        macro_rules! replace {
            ($($field:ident),*) => { $(
                doc.$field.retain(|decl| !affected(candidate, &decl.site));
                doc.$field.extend(replacement.$field.clone());
            )* };
        }
        replace!(tables, maintains, timelines, views, feed_views, effects, shapes, functions, programs, actions, routes, schedules);
        doc.source.extend(&replacement.source);
    }
    context
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    const MODEL: &str = "table members:\n  active: bool\n  cap 50\n  public\n";

    fn accepted(documents: &[Document], candidate: &Candidate) -> bool { check(documents, candidate) == Check::Accepted }

    fn candidate(nested: &str) -> (Document, Diagnostic, Candidate) {
        let source = format!("{MODEL}query q(c: cursor ?):\n  return members first 20 after c {{ twin: {nested} }}\n");
        let (document, mut diagnostics) = parser::parse_document("schema.q", &source);
        assert_eq!(diagnostics.len(), 1, "{diagnostics:?}");
        let diagnostic = diagnostics.remove(0);
        let candidate = document.source.repairs.iter().find(|r| r.rewrite == diagnostic.rewrite).unwrap().clone();
        (document, diagnostic, candidate)
    }

    fn suppressed(mut document: Document, mut diagnostic: Diagnostic, mut candidate: Candidate, broken: &str) {
        candidate.rewrite = broken.into();
        diagnostic.rewrite = broken.into();
        document.source.repairs = vec![candidate];
        let message = diagnostic.message.clone();
        let mut diagnostics = vec![diagnostic];
        verify(&[document], &mut diagnostics);
        assert_eq!(diagnostics[0].rewrite, "", "unchecked repair escaped verification");
        assert_eq!(diagnostics[0].message, message);
        assert_eq!(diagnostics[0].code, "E_QUARRY_SELF_EQUALITY");
    }

    #[test]
    fn broken_syntax_names_and_types_are_suppressed_before_offering() {

            let (document, diagnostic, candidate) = candidate("members where .id = .id");
//! Apply compiler-marked repairs, preserving surrounding source.

use crate::project::Project;
use snapback4_lang::{Bound, Diagnostic, lexer::{self, Kind, Token}};

/// The check command's source-I/O refusal, also exercised by the interleaved-editor subprocess tests.
pub(super) fn checked(result: std::io::Result<Bound>) -> Result<Bound, i32> {
    result.map_err(|error| { eprintln!("cannot read snapback/: {error}"); 2 })
}

// @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — --fix also opts into a context-verified positional-key choice; recompile each edit
pub fn compile(project: &Project) -> std::io::Result<Bound> {
    compile_observed(project, |_| {})
}

// The observer lets the integration suite interleave an editor save at each I/O boundary.
pub(super) fn compile_observed(project: &Project, mut observe: impl FnMut(&str)) -> std::io::Result<Bound> {
    let mut sources = project.sources()?;
    // A spelling repair removes at least one original token's ambiguity.
    // This also bounds a faulty diagnostic that keeps suggesting insertions.
    let budget = sources.iter().map(|(_, text)| text.chars().count()).sum::<usize>();
    for _ in 0..budget {
        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&sources);
        let repair = bound.diagnostics.iter().filter(|d| d.mechanical).find_map(|diagnostic| {
            let index = sources.iter().position(|(name, _)| name == &diagnostic.site.file)?;
            edit(&sources, index, diagnostic).map(|(target, start, end, replacement)| (target, start, end, replacement, diagnostic))
        });
        observe("compiled");
        let Some((target, start, end, replacement, diagnostic)) = repair else { return project.compile(); };
        let (name, source) = &sources[target];
        let path = project.schema_dir().join(name);
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#18.5 — a cross-file repair depends on every compiled source, including the query's file.
        verify_sources(project, &sources)?;
        let mut changed = source.clone();
        changed.replace_range(start..end, &replacement);
        if changed == *source { return project.compile(); }
        observe("write");
        // Best effort: an editor can still save between these reads and the write.
        verify_sources(project, &sources)?;
        std::fs::write(&path, &changed)?;
        show_diff(name, source, &changed);
        eprintln!("fix: {} — {}: {}", diagnostic.site, diagnostic.code, diagnostic.rewrite.replace('\n', " "));
        sources[target].1 = changed;
    }
    project.compile()
}

fn verify_sources(project: &Project, sources: &[(String, String)]) -> std::io::Result<()> {
    let current = project.sources()?;
    if let Some(name) = sources.iter().find(|(name, text)| !current.iter().any(|(n, t)| n == name && t == text)).map(|(name, _)| name)
        .or_else(|| current.iter().find(|(name, _)| !sources.iter().any(|(n, _)| n == name)).map(|(name, _)| name)) {
        return Err(changed_source(&project.schema_dir().join(name)));
    }
    Ok(())
}

fn changed_source(path: &std::path::Path) -> std::io::Error {
    std::io::Error::other(format!("{} changed while check --fix was running; rerun", path.display()))
}

pub(super) fn edit(sources: &[(String, String)], index: usize, diagnostic: &Diagnostic) -> Option<(usize, usize, usize, String)> {
    if !diagnostic.mechanical { return None; }
    let (_, source) = &sources[index];
    let start = offset(source, diagnostic.site.line, diagnostic.site.col)?;
    if matches!(diagnostic.code.as_str(), "E_QUARRY_TYPE" | "E_QUARRY_UNKNOWN" | "E_QUARRY_BOUND")
        && let Some((site, original)) = diagnostic.verified_edit() {
        let from = offset(source, site.line, site.col)?;
        let end = from.checked_add(original.len())?;
        return (source.get(from..end)? == original).then(|| (index, from, end, diagnostic.rewrite.clone()));
    }
    if diagnostic.code == "E_QUARRY_SYNTAX" && diagnostic.message == "shapes infer their row type; omit on table" {
        let actual = tokens(&source[start..]);
        let [on, table, equals, ..] = actual.as_slice() else { return None; };
        if on.value != "on" || table.kind != Kind::Word || equals.value != "=" { return None; }
        let end = start + offset(&source[start..], equals.site.line, equals.site.col)? + 1;
        return Some((index, start, end, diagnostic.rewrite.clone()));
    }
    if diagnostic.code == "E_REDUNDANT_RULE" && diagnostic.message.starts_with("aggregate read rule repeats its parent's rule through ") {
        let end = start + source[start..].find(['\n', '\r']).unwrap_or(source.len() - start);
        return Some((index, start, end, diagnostic.rewrite.clone()));
    }
    if diagnostic.code == "E_QUARRY_MODIFIER_SCOPE" {

import type { Refusal } from './contract.ts';

// @ref LLP 3000#http-overload-diagnostics — distinguish HTTP refusals from lost replies.
export function refusal(value: unknown): Refusal | undefined {
  if (!value || typeof value !== 'object') return;
  const r = value as Record<string, unknown>;
  if (!['code', 'family', 'message'].every(key => typeof r[key] === 'string' && r[key] !== '')) return;
  if (r.retryAfterMs !== undefined && (!Number.isSafeInteger(r.retryAfterMs) || Number(r.retryAfterMs) < 0)) return;
  if (r.retryable !== undefined && typeof r.retryable !== 'boolean') return;
  if (!['detail', 'site', 'rewrite', 'guide', 'rule'].every(key => r[key] === undefined || typeof r[key] === 'string')) return;
  return value as Refusal;
}
export const overloaded = (why?: Refusal): boolean => why?.code === 'E_RATE_LIMIT' && why.retryable === true;
export function transportRefusal(error?: unknown): Refusal {
  const failure = error as { code?: string; message?: string; cause?: { code?: string; message?: string } } | undefined;
  const cause = failure?.cause?.code ?? failure?.cause?.message ?? failure?.code ?? failure?.message;
  return { code: 'E_OFFLINE', family: 'link', message: `the request did not complete${cause ? `: ${cause}` : '; the host supplied no transport cause'}; check the connection and server logs before retrying`, retryable: true };
}
export function invalidResponse(status: number, outcome = 'request'): Refusal {
  return { code: 'E_HTTP_RESPONSE', family: 'link', message: `the server answered HTTP ${status} without a valid ${outcome} response; the ${outcome} outcome is unknown; inspect the server or proxy logs before retrying`, retryable: true };
}
export function retryAfter(value: string | null): number {
  if (!value) return 0;
  const seconds = /^\d+(?:\.\d+)?$/.test(value.trim()) ? Number(value) : NaN;
  const delay = Number.isNaN(seconds) ? Date.parse(value) - Date.now() : seconds * 1000;
  return Number.isNaN(delay) ? 0 : Math.max(0, delay);
}
function pause(ms: number, signal?: AbortSignal): Promise<void> {
  return new Promise(resolve => {
    if (signal?.aborted) { resolve(); return; }
    const done = () => { clearTimeout(timer); signal?.removeEventListener('abort', done); resolve(); };
    const timer = setTimeout(done, Math.min(ms, 2_147_483_647));
    signal?.addEventListener('abort', done, { once: true });
  });
}
export const jitter = (delay: number): number => delay * (1 + Math.random() * 0.25);
/** An unsuccessful probe never establishes identity, even if the peer replied. */
export function pauseProbe(response: Response | undefined, signal: AbortSignal): Promise<void> {
  return pause(jitter(Math.max(1000, retryAfter(response?.headers.get('retry-after') ?? null))), signal);
}
export async function readResponse(response: Response, decode?: (response: Response) => Promise<Record<string, unknown>>, onParsed?: (json: Record<string, unknown>) => void): Promise<Record<string, unknown>> {
  let json: Record<string, unknown>;

  catch (error) { if (!(error instanceof SyntaxError)) throw error; return { denied: invalidResponse(response.status) }; }

Each app uploadAndPlace intent owns one placement; use explicit uploads for several columns in one mutation. Or reference the original home row. See snapback4 guide uploads --section "Copy the same image".';
    const improved = { ...placement, message: `${placement.message}; ${recipe}` };

/** Only safe reads use this helper. Same-ID mutations retain their outbox's

export async function readWithAdmissionRetry(send: () => Promise<Response>, current: () => boolean, signal?: AbortSignal, decode?: (response: Response) => Promise<Record<string, unknown>>, changesStream = false) {

export async function pauseRefusal(response: Response, why: Refusal | undefined, signal: AbortSignal): Promise<boolean> {

  await pause(jitter(Math.max(1000, retryAfter(response.headers.get('retry-after')))), signal);

}

snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/language.rs
# Rules

**Snapback 2 optimizes the edit-to-verified loop — the agent's building an app on it, and ours building it.**

Speed is the constraint. Everything below exists to keep the edit-to-verified loop short
and to stop this repository accreting apparatus faster than anyone can remove it. These
rules bind `snapback2/` and the LLPs numbered 1000 and up; Snapback 1 is baselined, not
exempt, and its numbers may only fall.

Each rule is **[check]** (enforced by `caps`) or **[review]** (enforced by a human).

## Budgets — every budget is a trade, not a limit

- **5 blocking checks, 60s total.** A sixth requires deleting one, in the same PR. **[check]**
- **59 documents in the working set** (`llp/current/`). Today's count. A 60th requires
  archiving one — which is just removing the link. Snapback 2 aims to leave this at 15;
  Snapback 1's links are archived at promotion. **[check]**
- **12 documents in the foundation** (`llp/foundation/`). **[check]**
- **This file: 700 words.** If it grows, something becomes a check or stops being a rule. **[check]**
- **1,500 lines per source file, baseline 204.** Generated files exempt — and generated
  files are built, not committed; so are benchmark run records under `benchmarks/runs/`,
  which are evidence, not source. The baseline is Snapback 1's oversize count on the day
  the rule landed; it may not grow, and `snapback2/` contributes zero to it. **[check]**

## Blocking checks

`caps` · `ref-check` · `snapback2/scripts/test.sh` · `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs --check` · `check-inventories`

## Loop shape

- **Every check reports all failures in one run.** Fail-fast is banned. **[check]**
- **Nothing blocks on anything slower than 60 seconds.** Slow verification runs on a
  schedule, attributed to the breaking commit, and files a bounded set of issues. **[review]**
- **Fix loops get 3 rounds.** Then stop: escalate to a human or descope. **[review]**

## Time budgets

Tracked every run of `snapback2/scripts/measure.mjs` (appends `snapback2/metrics/loop.jsonl`).
A regression is a P0 with a name on it.

| | budget |
|---|---|
| Incremental rebuild after a one-line edit | 15s |
| Test what you just changed | 30s |
| Blocking gate suite | 60s |
| Full build, warm cache | 5 min |
| **`snapback2 check` on a program (the agent's product latency)** | **1s** |
| **`snapback2 doctor` boot-to-ready** | **0.5s** |

## Scope

- **`NOT-DOING.md` is binding.** Moving something onto the doing-list means writing why
  and taking something off. **[review]**
- **Delete; don't deprecate.** No compat shims or migration paths before promotion. **[review]**
- **Nothing is compiled, transpiled, or generated at runtime that could be built ahead of
  time.** **[review]**
- **A spec needs an implementer and a date, or it isn't written.** **[review]**
- **Only implemented specs bind.** A document with no running code behind it is a
  proposal, and a proposal cannot block a PR or justify a check. **[review]**

## Agents

- **Agents remove apparatus freely and add none.** An agent PR that adds a check, script,
  registry, config file, or design doc needs explicit human approval saying so. **[review]**
- **2026-09-06 session exception:** the user delegated the search implementation
  decisions; one `snapback2-core/build.rs` may generate pinned Unicode tables at
  build time, alongside the existing package build. No new gate, registry,
  runtime generator, or Unicode dependency is authorized by this exception. **[review]**
- **Snapback 2 LLPs are numbered 1000 and up**; `llp-create` must be told. **[review]**
- **Snapback 4 LLPs are numbered 3000 and up** (`snapback4/`, LLP 3000); it adopts these budgets
  and loop shape as its own and keeps its scope in `snapback4/NOT-DOING.md`. **[review]**

## Shared machines

- Warm, named lanes, one per stream of work. Never clone fresh; reuse the worktree.
- **Never `git stash`.** Use a temporary commit.
- Kill only PIDs you recorded at launch. Never by name or pattern.

    pub(super) fn expression(&mut self, minimum: u8, stops: &[&str]) -> Fallible<Expr> {
        let start = self.position;
        let mut left = self.prefix(stops)?;
        loop {
            let token = self.peek().clone();
            if self.is_stop(&token, stops) {
                break;
            }
            let Some(precedence) = precedence(&token.value) else { break };
            if precedence < minimum {
                break;
            }
            self.expression_spans.insert((left.site().line, left.site().col), start..self.position);
            self.position += 1;
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#19.3 — recover through guessed equality without admitting an alias or cascading through its block.
            let adjacent_equals = |next: &Token, col| next.kind == Kind::Symbol && next.value == "=" && next.site.line == token.site.line && next.site.col == col;
            if token.kind == Kind::Symbol && token.value == "=" && adjacent_equals(self.peek(), token.site.col + 1)
                && !adjacent_equals(self.peek_at(1), token.site.col + 2) {
                self.diagnostics.push(syntax_with(&token.site, "equality is = in Quarry", "=").mechanical());
                self.position += 1;
            }
            let right = self.expression(precedence + u8::from(token.value != "??"), stops)?;
            if precedence == 4 {
                self.comparison_spans.insert((token.site.line, token.site.col), start..self.position);
            }
            left = Expr::Binary { op: token.value, left: Box::new(left), right: Box::new(right), site: token.site };
            // Optional-filter guards admit the SQL/host-language equality
            // spellings locally; unrelated guessed == retains its repair.
            if let Some(sites) = optional_filter_equalities(&left) {
                self.diagnostics.retain(|d| !(d.message == "equality is = in Quarry" && sites.contains(&d.site)));
            }
        }
        if minimum == 0 && self.at("?") {
            let site = self.take("?")?.site;
            let yes = self.expression(0, &[":"])?;
            self.take(":")?;
            let no = self.expression(0, stops)?;
            return Err(unsupported(&site, "? :", format!("{} if {} else {}", print::expression(&yes), print::expression(&left), print::expression(&no))));
        }
        // `a if cond else b`, below every operator: the value is one of the
        // two, and the other is never evaluated.
        if minimum == 0 && self.at("if") && !stops.contains(&"if") && self.peek_at(1).value != "absent" {
            let site = self.take("if")?.site;
            let mut inner: Vec<&str> = stops.to_vec();
            inner.push("else");
            let condition = self.expression(0, &inner)?;
            if !self.maybe("else") {
                return Err(syntax_with(&site, "a conditional value takes both arms: value if condition else other", format!("{} if {} else null", print::expression(&left), print::expression(&condition))));
            }
            let otherwise = self.expression(0, stops)?;
            left = Expr::If { then: Box::new(left), condition: Box::new(condition), otherwise: Box::new(otherwise), site };
        }
        self.expression_spans.insert((left.site().line, left.site().col), start..self.position);
        self.document.source.record(&left, &self.source, &self.tokens[start..self.position]);
        Ok(left)
    }

    pub(super) fn prefix(&mut self, stops: &[&str]) -> Fallible<Expr> {

# What Snapback 4 Does Not Do

Longer than the doing-list on purpose. Moving something off it means writing one
line naming what it unblocks and taking something off the doing-list in the same
change. Nothing comes off during slice one (LLP 3000 §3: the clock is four weeks).

## The bars

The bakeoff gates in [LLP 3000 §3](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) are the
bars: the loop bar, hub acquisition within 2× the measured floor, client bytes
under the competitor-anchored budgets, the six scenarios green in the simulator
on the hub fixture. Everything not required to pass them does not exist yet.

## Not building

- **A second authoring language.** Quarry, one spelling, copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b). TypeScript is for seeds, journeys, effect bodies and the client.
- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
- **A device per-query result cache.** No range receipts, halos, absence evidence, coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, fact integrity or swap journals. The client replicates declared partitions with one watermark each (LLP 2000.000 §7). The server's narrow Postgres revision-equality cache and explicitly clock-qualified G6 read grant are the exceptions below; cross-revision promotion remains excluded.
- **Engine-minted ids for client inserts, grants, ID slices.** Ids are client-minted, time-ordered, 128-bit; the engine mints for its own writes.
- **The `drain` protocol, follower-tab inbox rows, `defineItems`, `withHistory`, `mutationTarget`, `receipt.source`, `local.caughtUp`, `partial` reason codes.** The card (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`) is the whole client vocabulary; `why` carries the rest.
- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
- **An embedded JS engine for effects.** The data plane never calls out. `emit` writes the effect durably in the mutation's commit and an external worker holding a service token claims, performs and settles it over the wire (`snapback4/worker`); the four reference effects run on the builder's Node (LLP 2000.000 §10, decided 2026-09-13).
- **Committed generated code; an auth mode; a supervised bundler.** As Snapback 2 (`rules/NOT-DOING.md`): types by inference, personas not modes, `SNAPBACK_URL` is the client contract.
- **A TypeScript twin of the interpreter.** Retired by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13 (LLP 3000 §7): one implementation, in Rust, on every host — the core's SQLite under the native module, memory under wasm on the web with the host persisting each commit's changes. The web pays 312,924 B gzip before the diet where the twin's tier paid 16,842; the conformance obligation between two interpreters ends. Expo Go runs the online tier.
- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
- **General observability beyond `why`, `trace`, `logs`, `data`, `sql`, server operation latency, and bounded client subscription inspection.** The `sql` verb is read-only inspection of the developer's own store (LLP 3000.007 §8.8), never an application surface. Separately declared application SQL reads are admitted for increment D (LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4; the 2026-09-16 Moves line below), not implemented by this inspection verb.
- **A guide corpus.** The compiler's facts, one language card and keyword cards, as Snapback 2; no freshness dates. Every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite and the language card is run, so a card cannot promise a form the parser refuses.

- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
- **Exact horizon maintenance.** The horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a bootstrap); the log never evicts or back-fills to hold a device's group at exactly N rows. A local read past the acquired horizon by key says `complete: false`.

- **Clock expressions in rules.** A read rule compares with `=` and `!=` only; `.expiresAt > now` remains a mutation `require`. Stored lifetimes use `not before .col` for server-controlled opening and `expire at .col` for expiry. The engine publishes opening commits; devices receive no unopened payload. The supported compositions and explicit exclusions are in LLP 3000.036.003.
- **A jobs framework.** No priorities, worker pools or dead letters: one FIFO of runs, one chunk at a time, a refused chunk parked with its refusal. Actions, effects and scheduled actions carry per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation, and a record per external step (LLP 3000.009 §5.2; the Moves line below). Missed period boundaries while a server is down fire once on return, not once per missed period.
- **Asset processing.** No transcoding, thumbnails, rungs or HEIC: the bytes are served as uploaded; JPEG, PNG, WebP and MP4 only; image dimensions and optional MP4 duration read from the header; MP4 dimensions are not extracted.
- **Search beyond word prefixes.** One tokenizer (NFC, lowercase, alphanumeric words), one index shape (`scope, words(cols), order.., id`), one read (`matching`): every term a prefix of some word, the first term's words merged as lanes, bounded by the meter in a uniform scope, at most 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope. No stemming, no ranking by relevance, no phrase or mid-word matching, no per-language folding, no intersection index for the second term (it filters the page).
- **An identity provider.** Guests, password accounts, service seats, and sessions a service mints for accounts the application authenticated itself. No phone, OAuth, magic links or MFA in the engine: an application's worker verifies those and mints the session.
- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.

## Process not doing

- **2026-09-28 — ordinary mixed search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3, lane OS round 1).
  Admit the SDK's bounded merge of current server rows and certified Left images
  for the proved single time-ordered word page. This removes application-owned
  online/held selection for that shape. One server call per requested page;
  recomputation pays again from consumed positions. The coordinator shares the
  existing device read allowance. No durable search session, extra synced rows,
  new authority or additional allowance is admitted.

- **2026-09-28 — inferred current-scope word search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3,
  lane OS). A single bare ordinary word page may derive the exact non-expiring
  membership audience for the existing compact server plan. This removes the
  requirement to repeat that audience predicate in this narrow server query.
  The compiler supplies a scoped words index when absent; it is an ordinary
  schema index, maintained on writes and on devices, with no extra synced rows
  or new authority. The device keeps its original bare scan. Round 0 left ordered composition
  and combined accounting outside its independent subset; round 1 above admits
  that bounded SDK composition. Durable search sessions remain excluded.
- **2026-10-12 — union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000.000, workstream UNION, two blind review rounds). A table syncs to the union of principal-column audiences and one group audience; a device holds a row while any branch admits its viewer. What it unblocks: owner-or-team, buyer-or-seller and owner-or-staff tables offline for every reader, the shape three app farms asked for. Taken off: "one `sync` audience per table". Partial delivery, two groups and a public branch stay excluded (above).
- **2026-09-28 — explicit owner-key coverage** (LLP 3000.028 §3.4, Lane OK commission). A public window may opt into a compiler-owned, horizon-free principal key partition to answer ordinary own-key existence offline. Taken off: the exclusion of narrower platform key distribution beside one public window. General union audiences, full own-row replication and per-query absence caches remain excluded. The projection uses existing metered maintains, adoption and whole-partition publication; existing windows do not expand.
- **2026-09-26 — a live query keeps presence current** (LLP 3000.018). A 0.4.2 probe found an observed `connected[p]` reporting `fresh: true` while stale for over a minute, and a comparative builder polled around it. What it unblocks: online marks and presence-aware lists that stay current. Taken off: "a presence stream" as an exclusion for live queries; a presence API, last-seen storage and multi-server presence stay out. Under the author's standing authorization for reworks that make queries excellent (2026-09-25).

- **No drift registries, inventories, line-pinned diagnostics registries, claim digests or frozen-record gates.** Generated projections are built, not committed.
- **No gate bound to an exact commit; no push gated on a battery.** Five blocking checks in sixty seconds, run before push; everything slower on a schedule with bounded tickets.
- **No ceiling raised in slice one.** Budgets are anchored to a competitor's measured number and recorded once in the journal.
- **No standing panel** except the two blind reviews LLP 3000 decision 4 names (partition inversion; the meter's cut on non-uniform scans). The author explicitly commissioned the one-off LLP 3000.008 panel and disjoint Astra implementation lanes on 2026-09-14; its bounded disposition is recorded in that umbrella and LLP 3000 §7.
- **No new LLP number** without first extending LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000; no spec without running code (`rules/RULES.md`: only implemented specs bind).
- **No second session on the core** during slice one; lanes take the periphery.
- **No test matrix as the primary harness.** The simulator is; real-browser and device witnesses cover only what it cannot reach.
- **No files over 1,500 lines**, including ones copied from Snapback 2. Split on
  the way in. The one exception is the package's published documents
  (`packages/snapback4/README.md` and its linked references): the rule exists to
  keep a *source* file reviewable, and the 0.2.25 calibration measured the cost of
  applying it to a document — splitting the README was that run's single largest
  scoring loss, because the builder then had four places to look instead of one
  (LLP 3000.007 §20.19). A published document is bounded by whether a builder can
  find one way in, not by a line count; the census that enforces this rule covers
  source, and says so.

## Deliberately worse

- No compatibility with Snapback 1 or Snapback 2 apps, packages or on-disk formats.
- No API stability before the clock; every publish is `0.0.x`.
- Sparse prose documentation; the diagnostics and the recipes carry the load.
- One hosted target, one platform binary at a time. SQLite remains the default store; Postgres deploy/serve admits the initial Unix-socket HTTP profile under LLP 3000.009.001 (Moves, 2026-10-20; `guide release-json`). Provider HA, full parity and publication remain outside that profile.
