## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,
`dev`, `check`, `doctor`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `trace`, `guide`; H0
`deploy`, `rollback`, `secret`; channels; assets; the word-prefix index.

**Flip 1 — certify authorization, meter resources** (LLP 2000.000 §6). The
certificate keeps every read site's shape, uniformity, completeness class
and prediction eligibility, and stops pricing bytes and steps. A
deterministic meter charges examined rows, index entries, rule probes,
steps, heap, output and rows written as the program runs, on the server and
in the client interpreter alike. A query cut by the meter returns `capped`
with no continuation past the cut, the same observable outcome as a static
ceiling cut today; a mutation aborts with `E_BOUND` naming actual work; a
job is chunked. `check --cost` prints the structural cost class; `test`
prints measured meters on the hub fixture.

**Flip 2 — the viewer's partition is the unit of sync** (LLP 2000.000 §7).
`sync to <audience> last N by <index>` on a table makes the engine maintain,
per principal, the rows that principal may read, as a change log with a
horizon; a table without the word is online-only, and `check` says which
not make an unchanged publication checkout dirty. Job guide tests retain backlog
catch-up before the exclusive coalesced period end.

SECP's final conflict-correction identity is .005: a read-only check of excluded
SEC11b found provisional .003/.004 reservations after the first integration
assignment. Historical .001 reviews and the intermediate .003 commit remain in
history. SEC11b's .002 conflict with landed SEC12 is a filesystem follow-up; none
of that excluded branch's new code or documents is merged.

### 2026-10-27 — Integration 28 isolated social fixture custody

The complete installed consumer chain exposed the social optional-upload fixture’s
nested 30-second spawn timeout under proot: it killed a check that had already
produced its valid result after 26 seconds. Use the existing owned toolchain
deadline and process-group drain instead of a second inner wall-clock fuse.
Exact zero exit, current JSON, stock/edited-source optional-upload assertions and
no decision-file assertion remain; the existing five-minute command and twenty-minute
starter guards, fixed security work budgets and measured latency ticket are unchanged.


## 2026-10-27 — OWN: silent ownership refusal after restart

A deterministic private-PG16/17 public-service witness reproduces six warm-up
ACKs followed by a durable but unconfirmed seventh write, a sticky G6 admission
pause, repeated `E_STORE_OWNERSHIP` and a still-renewing lease. The synchronous
public owner now observes terminal admission uncertainty in its independent
watchdog and exits; it leaves the lease/read epoch to expire before ordinary
takeover. The gate reports the first pause reason before publishing its flag.
Arm E retains its fenced recovery. No uncertain invocation is automatically
replayed or old cache epoch reopened. See
`reports/20261027-ownership-loss/REPORT.md` for baseline/fix evidence, tests and
the limits of attribution to the two original incidents.

## 2026-10-27 — OWN review fixes: bounded diagnostics and serving-only G6 exit

The first OWN implementation could wedge before publishing loss when stderr
stalled, and armed its G6 process exit for short-lived deploy before activation
reconciliation. Loss now precedes bounded diagnostic queueing; queued replies
drain without logging I/O, and exit allows at most 100 ms for diagnostic output.
Only `serving()` arms the G6 exit. Deploy retains persisted-selection recovery,
pointer/log completion and lease release. The report's new regressions fill a
real stderr pipe and delay an unknown-but-durable activation's reconciliation
probe. LLP 3000.009.001.000 §5.1 records the corrected lifecycle contract.

## 2026-10-27 — CALLS1: image metadata and a quiet install join

Wave 34 file options selected the document path on image-only schemas. The client now retries only the explicit pre-body no-file-column refusal through the existing media sniffer, using the same bytes and credentials; mixed/file schemas keep document semantics. Image bounds, placement and authorization remain enforced. The installer retains its private worker custody, puts npm chatter in its private log and gives --wait one final completion line; init promotes that blocking join instead of status polling. No version bump.

CALLS1's explicitly requested MIME/name/size upload shape adds only optional advisory `UploadOptions.file.size`; the server still measures the bytes and the existing result/authority contract is unchanged. This is the scoped upload option acceptance authorized by the brief, not a client-contract redesign.

## 2026-10-27 — CALLS1: context-verified diagnostic edits

Quarry fix-its preserve source spans, compile the affected operation before offering an edit, and print a diff when applied. Optional bool conditions compare with true; nullable arithmetic propagates null or guards a positive condition, retaining the authored refusal code. Required insert columns are copied only from same-named parameters, never inferred owners/defaults. Whole-population bounds require structural non-null uniqueness before first 1; nullable unique ties remain advisory. The guard regression also covers nested arithmetic whose source map records only the containing comparison. Existing race/editor checks remain in the fixer. Runtime IR and security binder rules are untouched.

A fresh Codex install replay found that its tool reclaimed init's unjoined background installer before the receipt existed. Init now owns the frontend installer through completion and returns its exit status. The backend-only mirror install measured 1.180 seconds; the reading/map output precedes frontend installation, npm chatter stays private, and explicitly deferred installs can still be started/joined separately. The before-correction transcript is retained as a failed preliminary witness, not a final success claim.

//! Assets on the wire: `POST /assets` takes the bytes and answers with the
//! asset's facts; `GET /assets/<id>` serves them to a viewer the home row's
//! read rule admits (LLP 1006 carried into Snapback 4). A one-view image is
//! served once per principal and never cached anywhere; a deleted, cleared
//! or expired home stops the bytes at the server. A `file` (LLP 3000.038)
//! is uploaded with `?as=file&name=` and its declared `Content-Type`, which
//! the server verifies against the bytes; it is only ever served as an
//! attachment under `nosniff` and a sandboxing CSP.

use crate::serve::{Shared, cors, RequestFacts, principal_of, refusal_json, respond};
use serde_json::json;
use snapback4_core::error::Refusal;
use std::io::{self, Read as _, Seek, SeekFrom};
use tiny_http::{Header, Request, Response};

/// The most an upload may be when no column bounds it yet: the request is
/// refused before the schema is consulted.
pub const UPLOAD_CEILING: usize = 64 << 20;

pub struct UploadPermit<'a>(&'a std::sync::atomic::AtomicUsize);
pub fn admit(shared: &Shared) -> Option<UploadPermit<'_>> {
    use std::sync::atomic::Ordering;
    shared.uploads.fetch_update(Ordering::AcqRel, Ordering::Acquire, |n| (n < 2).then_some(n + 1)).ok()?;
    Some(UploadPermit(&shared.uploads))
}
impl Drop for UploadPermit<'_> {
    fn drop(&mut self) { self.0.fetch_sub(1, std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Release); }
}

/// Whether `POST /assets` carries `as=file`.
pub fn is_file_upload(url: &str) -> bool {
    crate::serve::query_params(url).get("as").is_some_and(|kind| kind == "file")
}

/// The most bytes a file upload may stream: the schema's largest file
/// bound, under the general upload ceiling. A schema with no file column
/// refuses before any body is read.
pub fn file_ceiling(shared: &Shared) -> Result<usize, Refusal> {
    match shared.engine.call(|engine, _| engine.file_upload_ceiling())? {
        Some(max) => Ok((max as usize).min(UPLOAD_CEILING)),
        None => Err(Refusal::new("E_ASSET_TYPE", "asset", "no column of this backend takes a file; declare one (`doc: file <=10000000`)").guide("assets")),
    }
}

pub fn upload(request: Request, body: Vec<u8>, shared: &Shared) {
    let principal = match principal_of(&request, shared) {
        Ok(principal) => principal.0,
        Err(refusal) => return respond(request, 401, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&refusal) })),
    };
    if body.is_empty() {
        return respond(request, 400, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&Refusal::new("E_ASSET_TYPE", "asset", "the upload is empty: POST the file's bytes as the body").guide("assets")) }));
    }
    let params = crate::serve::query_params(request.url());
    let file = match params.get("as").map(String::as_str) {
        None | Some("image" | "video") => None,
        Some("file") => Some(params.get("name").cloned().unwrap_or_default()),
        Some(other) => return respond(request, 400, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("as={other} is not an upload kind; use as=file for a document, or omit it for an image or video")).guide("assets")) })),
    };
    let declared = match crate::serve::single_header(&request, "content-type") {

Decide evidence presence from the specification before wiring an upload. In the
schema below, `invariant documents always .scan != null` states permanent presence.
Use `invariant documents.insert only .scan != null` for creation-only presence;
later redaction also needs a nullable column. The generated security draft asks
this question. An optional creating argument that contradicts this intent requires
an individual security review. A required photo on a separate child does not
require a child row: declare the parent's evidence requirement and its permitted
draft states too. Deliberately optional attachments stay advisory.

Uploads are bound to the uploader: the account that places an asset must upload
its bytes. One upload has one home; reusing it in another row refuses. To share
evidence, reference its original row rather than placing its asset again. Required
workflow evidence needs a required mutation argument as well as a required column;
a conditional child insertion does not require evidence on its parent.


## Copy the same image into several columns

One home means one table/row/**column**, even when two columns share a row.
The account placing the image must upload it; an operator cannot lend its upload
to a different user. Upload the selected bytes once per destination column.
Asset IDs are placement identities, not content hashes: the two uploads below
get distinct IDs. Shared bytes without separate placements should instead use
references to the original readable home row.

```js
// `client` is signed in as the account calling the placing mutation.
// `bytes` is the same selected image; api.open is your own two-image mutation.
const first = await client.upload(bytes);
if (!first.ok) throw Error(first.why.message);
const second = await client.upload(bytes);
if (!second.ok) throw Error(second.why.message);
const write = await client.mutate(api.open, {
  front: {id: first.asset.id}, back: {id: second.asset.id},
});
if (write.state === "failed") throw Error(write.why.message);
```

For durable App uploads, each `uploadAndPlace` intent owns one placement.
For several columns in one mutation use the two explicit uploads above. Retrying a failed mutation with the same ID is
separate from creating a second home. A fresh placement never transfers uploader
or home authority. E_ASSET_PLACED carries this copy recipe in SDK responses.

## Placement replay: documents and one home

Save this complete schema as `snapback/schema.q`; run `npx snapback4 check`.
This local-only recipe tests ownership with personas on a disposable store.
Shared previews use real users via `guide staging`. Each intended copy uploads
fresh bytes as the account that places it. Shared evidence instead references
the original document row, whose current read rule still controls its bytes.

```quarry
use personas alice, bob
table documents:
  owner: principal
  scan: file <=10000 ('application/pdf')
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  online only
table copies:
  owner: principal
  source: documents
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  online only
mutation place(scan: file <=10000 ('application/pdf')):
  return insert documents {owner: viewer, scan}
mutation reference(source: documents):
  row = documents[source] ! else NOT_FOUND
  return insert copies {owner: viewer, source: row.id}
```

Save as `placements.mjs` beside snapback/; run `node placements.mjs` (Node 24).
The file hint selects document verification even when the bytes have a PDF
header. Image/video media omit it. Raw HTTP uploads can return HTTP 200 with
`{denied}`: inspect the envelope, or use the SDK as below. Upload succeeds with
`{ok:true,asset}`; download returns a URL handle or `{denied}`, never a Blob.

```js
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import { startDev } from "snapback4/proof";
import { createClient } from "snapback4/client";
import { api } from "./snapback/generated/api.ts";
const host = await startDev({args:["--memory", "--no-watch"]});
const alice = createClient({url:host.url, persona:"alice"});
const bob = createClient({url:host.url, persona:"bob"});
try {
  const bytes = new TextEncoder().encode("%PDF-1.7\nexample\n%%EOF\n");
  const missingHint = await alice.upload(bytes);
  assert.equal(missingHint.ok, false);
  assert.match(missingHint.why.message, /file/);
  const uploaded = await alice.upload(bytes, {file:{name:"report.pdf", type:"application/pdf"}});
  assert.equal(uploaded.ok, true);
  const scan = {id:uploaded.asset.id};
  const first = await alice.mutate(api.place, {scan});
  assert.equal(first.state, "sent");
  const reused = await alice.mutate(api.place, {scan});
  assert.equal(reused.why.code, "E_ASSET_PLACED");
  const foreign = await bob.mutate(api.place, {scan});
  assert.equal(foreign.why.code, "E_ASSET_UNKNOWN");
  assert.equal((await alice.mutate(api.reference, {source:first.result.id})).state, "sent");
  const read = await alice.asset(scan.id);
  if ("denied" in read) throw Error(read.denied.message);
  try { assert.equal(read.type, "application/pdf"); } finally { read.revoke(); }
} finally { alice.close(); bob.close(); await host.stop(); }
```

Place an upload within one hour. Time travel also collects unplaced bytes:
advance first, sign in again if needed, then upload and place. Missing, foreign,
collected and hidden assets share E_ASSET_UNKNOWN; the refusal never reveals
another account or a hidden home's contents. A same-uploader E_ASSET_PLACED
names the upload's existing home. `client.assetSource(id)` carries auth headers
for native downloads; browser save-on-demand uses `saveAsset(client,id)`.

## Proof signatures on demand

`guide api proof` is generated from the package declarations and fits 3,000 tokens.
Use it for host, account, browser and bounded serve checks.
The complete declarations live in dist/proof.d.ts; only follow a named type when
the digest does not answer your question. `guide production` owns the real-user
serve recipe, and the sections above own acceptance and day-two assertions.
# LLP 3000.037.000: Wave-30 default verdict and password starters

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Implemented
**Systems:** Snapback 4 CLI, column write authority, proof kit, starters
**Author:** Charlie Cheever / Codex
**Date:** 2026-10-26
**Revised:** 2026-10-27 (CALLS1 §9); 2026-10-27
**Related:** LLP 3000.037, LLP 3000.036.004, LLP 3000.036.004.000, LLP 3000.011.003.000.000

## 1. Default verdict

Wave 30's 033 F1 had a concrete campaign witness, but its builder never opened
`--review`. Both default check and changed deploy search the existing bounded
question campaign and print every available unacknowledged witness with its
question. This is an actionable fix question, not an authored invariant
refutation or an additional gate. Gate semantics remain SEC10's responsibility.
Deploy retains witnessed virtual `$derived/` source sites even when ordinary
changed-source filtering cannot map them to an authored filename.
The check verdict and deploy’s leading verdict count gates, witnessed questions and advisories;
advisories explicitly do not block. Unwitnessed advice remains behind --review.
N_WRITE_DENIED, N_REQUIRE_HIDDEN, N_NEVER_COMPLETE and N_UNREADABLE_PROBE are
an actionable tier shown by default. The expanded security-question block leads with gates, then
witnessed questions, then other advice. A disposition lacking a witness says
that its specification rationale has not been validated by replay. Unsupported
codes say witness unavailable; supported exhausted searches say none obtained.
Neither wording promises that the acknowledged specification is true.

Existing redaction, source binding, work caps and structured evidence survive.
The existing generated cache is an optimization, never authority. A cold check
now pays the bounded witness search; measurements must include cold latency.
No witness is not evidence of safety. Default output targets median <=5 lines
for both check and deploy on the fifty wave-30 schemas; individual actionable
findings can exceed it. Deploy prints its newline-terminated security notice
without adding a blank line. Enforcement prose and JSON evidence remain intact.

## 2. Default initialization

LLP 3000.036.004.000 §10 supersedes its original "defaults count" rule.
A null or a value equal to a declared literal default initializes a column at
insert and requires no column write grant. Explicitly supplying the same
literal is equivalent to omission. This applies to insert-if-absent and the
insert arm of upsert. Table insert authority always applies. A non-default
insert value requires the grant. Changed updates, including resetting a value
to its default and edits via upsert, retain old-and-next grant checks.
Dynamic `now` and `viewer` defaults retain ordinary column authority; this
amendment grants only literal initialization, with no new wire expression.
Authored column-write intent and enforced adversarial contracts use that same
initialization predicate.
Authored whole-row presence/approval assertions remain independent obligations.

## 3. Password online starters

`init --web --online --password` and the Expo equivalent create a small notes
app with password sign-in, operator-only provision, online tables and writes,
no device initialization, no personas, and no todo fixtures. `--password`
also selects this shape for a new schema without requiring --online. Beside an
existing schema init preserves it and validates an explicit password request.
The example's real member/admin accounts are provisioned before frontend proofs;
new public signups have no role and cannot create notes until provisioned.
Custom schemas still require application-specific write proofs.

## 8. Wave-32 builder reading and waiting (TOK1)

The start card replaces the introductory card tour with one complete compiling
customer/staff/evidence/deadline/approval schema and on-demand feature sections.
Its first read fits 8,000 UTF-8 bytes, a conservative 8,000 byte-token ceiling.
Default card output has the same hard byte budget; references remain available
with --full or an explicit section. Repeated project-local reads show only new
paragraphs or complete code fences; --repeat forces selected content and --full
forces the reference. The cache contains content hashes only, is disposable,
and never affects check, security or execution. Changed content is shown again.

Build-time declaration digests for proof/client/auth derive from dist declarations,
with a generated API operation index derived from the bound schema. Digests are
bounded, identify omitted declarations, and are checked for declaration drift.
Every starter script points to the relevant digest. Init prints start before
starting an asynchronous frontend install with private progress/status files,
a join/status command and normal npm exit status. A request identity is published
before spawning, so delayed startup cannot join an earlier success. An IPC-owned
worker group stops npm and its process-group descendants if the installer is killed; progress
and custody files are private. A compact file custody map
and the generated API index reduce starter rereads.

Probe scripts announce their indefinite lifetime before deployment. A project
receipt records a held run's lifetime and request activity without credentials;
a previously terminated run held over 60 seconds with zero requests refuses
before deployment unless --hold or SNAPBACK4_PROBE_HOLD=1 is supplied.
Unattended foreground first runs also stop after a 60-second idle grace, with
bounded host cleanup and the probe:check recipe. Check-owned serveCheck runs set the hold override because their uptime is
already bounded by the checker. Explicit hold preserves the
harness's indefinite lifetime contract; the external bakeoff harness must pass that opt-in (SNAPBACK4_PROBE_HOLD=1).
This is a developer-wait safeguard, never an authorization or deployment gate.

Asset placement retains one home per asset (including distinct columns of the
same row), and placement by the uploader. E_ASSET_PLACED includes a concrete
same-bytes upload-per-column recipe. The uploads card demonstrates that recipe
and distinguishes shared references from separate placements. No security binder
or client contract changes are required. Evidence and independent reviews are
recorded with the workstream report; no publication or version bump is made.


TOK1 review records preserve every received result: [Muse round 1](reviews/3000.037.000-tok1-muse-round1.md)
and [fresh Codex round 1](reviews/3000.037.000-tok1-codex-round1.md) found
check-owned hold, cross-card context and installer custody/join/privacy issues;

                None => format!("{} has no cap over this prefix; this position needs a bounded page", read.table),
            };
            return Err(if position == Position::Domain && self.caller_kind != ProgramKind::Query {
                Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_FANOUT", format!("{} needs a bound over the whole population it writes; an atomic mutation cannot page its fanout", read.table), format!("declare a real product limit `cap N{columns}` on {}, fitting the {ceiling} examined ceiling, or move this work into a resumable job (guide jobs); a first-N preview cannot stand in for every row", read.table)).guide("bounds")
            } else { Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_BOUND", message, rewrite).guide("bounds") });
        };
        if !read.seeks.is_empty() && read.order_keys.is_empty() && !matches!(position, Position::Aggregate | Position::Exists) && !(position == Position::Domain && self.caller_kind == ProgramKind::Job) {
            return Err(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_BOUND", "a seek needs an explicit page bound", format!("{} first 50 by {}", self.catalog.source.expression(&A::Read(read.clone())), index.name)).guide("bounds"));
        }

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct IndexDecl {
    pub name: String,
    /// The columns, in order, the `words(..)` component left out.
    pub columns: Vec<String>,
    pub unique: bool,
    pub refusal: Option<(String, Option<String>)>,
    /// `words(a, b)`: its position among the components (the number of
    /// columns before it) and the text columns it tokenizes. An index with
    /// one serves `matching` reads whose keys are exactly those columns,
    /// and nothing else.
    pub words: Option<(usize, Vec<String>)>,
    pub computed: std::collections::BTreeMap<String, Expr>,
    pub site: Site,
}

impl IndexDecl {
    /// Whether the index serves ordinary prefix reads and probes.
    pub fn is_plain(&self) -> bool {
        self.words.is_none() && self.computed.is_empty()
    }

    /// Whether a `matching` read with this many keys reads this index.
    pub fn searches_after(&self, keys: usize) -> bool {
        self.words.as_ref().is_some_and(|(position, _)| *position == keys)
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]
pub enum Effect {
    Read,
    Insert,
    Update,
    Delete,
//! One recognition of point keys versus prefix populations, shared by reads,
//! loop domains, merge discovery and program existence tests.
use super::*;

#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub(super) enum Position { Page, Domain, Aggregate, Exists }

impl Binder<'_> {
    pub(super) fn is_page(&self, read: &ReadExpr, point: ReadMode) -> Fallible<bool> {
        let global = read.keys.is_empty() && self.catalog.tables.get(&read.table)
            .and_then(|table| table.maintained.as_ref()).is_some_and(|maintain| maintain.by.is_empty());
        if !read.order_keys.is_empty() || read.range.is_some() || read.matching.is_some() || read.filter.is_some() || !read.seeks.is_empty() || read.keys.is_empty() && !global { return Ok(true); }
        if global { return Ok(false); }
        // Speculation cannot emit setup or consume compiler names. The selected
        // form binds its key expressions exactly once in the caller's body.
        let mut probe = self.clone();
        let info = probe.table(&read.table, &read.site)?;
        let mut lookup = read.clone();
        if point == ReadMode::FilteredPoint && info.maintained.is_some() && lookup.index.is_none() { lookup.index = Some("byGroup".into()); }
        match probe.select_index(info, &lookup, point, &mut setup::Body::default()) {
            Ok(_) => Ok(false),
            Err(error) if error.code == "E_QUARRY_AMBIGUOUS" => Err(error),
            Err(point_error) => {
                // Only an actual index prefix changes the form. A mistyped
                // full key or an uncovered named equality keeps its point
                // diagnostic, including the established repair.
                let mut probe = self.clone();
                match probe.select_index(info, read, ReadMode::Range, &mut setup::Body::default()) {
                    Ok(_) => Ok(true),
                    Err(error) if error.code == "E_QUARRY_AMBIGUOUS" => Err(error),
                    Err(error) if error.code == "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN" => Err(error),
                    Err(_) => Err(point_error),
                }
            },
        }
    }

    pub(super) fn prefix_bound(&self, read: &ReadExpr, index: &ast::IndexDecl, position: Position) -> Fallible<(ast::RangeSpec, Bound)> {
        if let Some(range) = &read.range { return Ok((range.clone(), Bound::Literal(range.take))); }
        let ceiling = if self.caller_kind == ProgramKind::Job { snapback4_core::Ceilings::job() } else { snapback4_core::Ceilings::default() }.examined;
        let pinned = &index.columns[..read.keys.len()];
        // One group per distinct source key: a parent-scoped source cap bounds
        // its aggregate rows too, without copying a constraint onto the view.
        // A pinned unique key bounds the group as a cap does (wave 3 #7); `.id = x`
        // plans as the closed seek `.id >= x and .id <= x` on the next column.
        let mut equal = pinned.to_vec();
        if let Some(next) = index.columns.get(read.keys.len()) {
            let printed = |op: &str| read.seeks.iter().filter(|seek| &seek.column == next && seek.op == op).map(|seek| crate::print::expression(&seek.value)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
            let (low, high) = (printed(">="), printed("<="));
            if read.seeks.len() == 2 && low.len() == 1 && low == high { equal.push(next.clone()); }
        }
        let cap = self.schema.group_bound(&read.table, pinned).into_iter().chain(crate::bind::unique_bound::unique_bound(self.schema, &read.table, &equal)).min().or_else(|| {
            let m = self.schema.maintain_for(&read.table)?;
            let parent = m.parent_key.as_ref()?;
            pinned.contains(&parent.reference).then(|| self.schema.group_bound(&m.source, &[parent.reference.clone()])).flatten()
        });
        let bound = if position == Position::Exists { Bound::Literal(1) }
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.22 — ruling 24: the schema cap supplies the whole group's ordinary literal scan bound.
        // Queries with a non-uniform rule keep this existing capped path;
        // scan_at upgrades uniformly decided aggregates to streaming below.
        else if position == Position::Aggregate && self.caller_kind == ProgramKind::Query && let Some(cap) = cap.filter(|cap| u64::from(*cap) <= ceiling) { Bound::Literal(cap) }
        else if position == Position::Aggregate || (position == Position::Domain && self.caller_kind == ProgramKind::Job) { Bound::Meter }
      let waiting = !("denied" in result.read) && !("loading" in result.read && link === "offline") && await queryCustody.blocked(op.name, result); checkRead(signal); return () => {
        if (waiting) return link === "offline" ? { denied: offline() } : { wait: async (signal: AbortSignal) => { await queryCustody.wait(op.name, result, signal); waiting = await queryCustody.blocked(op.name, result); } };
        if (answers.blocked(result) !== undefined || "data" in result.read && readDeadlineWithdrawn(matchingReads(watches.values(), op, args), result.serverExpiry)) { answers.serverAnswers.forget(`${op.name}:${JSON.stringify(args)}`); return; }
        if (publishLocal(result, () => true)) return "loading" in result.read && (link === "offline" || result.heldUnavailable) ? { denied: link === "offline" ? offline() : heldQueryRefusal() } : result.read;
      };
    }, () => terminalQueryRefusal({ scope: null, store: null, closed: closed ? unavailable() : null, session: sessionDead ? unavailable() : null, changes: changesStopped, partition: partitionDenied, inherited: null }), aborter.signal),
    observe<A, R>(op: Query<A, R>, args: A, options?: ObserveOptions): Observed<R> {
      validateObserveOptions(options);
      const keep = options?.keep;
      const key = observationKey(op, args, options);
      return observeWatch(watches, key,
        () => Object.assign(localWatch(keep, op as Query<unknown, unknown>, args, key, incarnation, pendingRead), { opaque: () => !backend?.programs.some(p => p.name === op.name && !p.native) }),
        watch => {
          watchEphemeral(watch); const d = watch.deadlines;
          // @ref LLP 3000.023#3.4 — resubscription resumes the displayed row's original deadlines.
          if (d) scheduleExpiry(watch, d.device, d.server, d.localWakeup, d.valueDevice, d.valueServer);
          answers.resume(watch); void scheduleRead(watch); },
        watch => { answers.detach(watch); readWork.cancel(watch); ephemeralReads.remove(watch); }, unavailable, false, watch => { watch.dirty = false; return readWork.cancel(watch); },
        (watch, detached) => answers.restore(watch, detached, unavailable() ?? changesStopped ?? partitionDenied));
    },
    mutate: routeMutations(ephemeral.mutate, function<A, R>(op: Mutation<A, R>, args: A, options?: MutateOptions): Promise<Write<R>> {
      const id = options?.id === undefined ? mintId() : options.id;
      const diagnose = (why: Refusal) => observeRefusal(op.name, proofIdentity(), why);
      const unavailableWhy = unavailable() ?? (partitionDenied?.code === "E_STORE_RESTORE" ? partitionDenied : null);
      if (unavailableWhy) {
        diagnose(unavailableWhy);
        const failed = { state: "failed" as const, id, why: unavailableWhy };
        if (!mutationIds.knows(id)) emitLocalFailure(failed);
        return failedAdmission<R>(id, unavailableWhy);
      }
      return followingHistory.track(op.name, () => followingIntents.track(op.name, () => mutationIds.run<R>(id, op.name, args, () => client.write(id), (args, pending) => {
        const waiting = !identified || identification.size > 0;
        const identity = waiting ? localAdmissionIdentity(backend, generation) : undefined;
        const submit = (release = () => {}): Promise<Write<R>> => {
        const request = { op: "admit", ...(waiting ? identity!.fields() : {}),
          entry: mutationEntry(id, ++submissions, op.name, args, principal, clock.issuedDeviceNow(), backend!, options?.id !== undefined) };
        const denied = admitNow(core, request);
        if (denied) { diagnose(denied); release(); if (denied.code === "E_ADMISSION_STORE") emitLocalFailure({ state: "failed", id, why: denied }); return failedAdmission<R>(id, denied); }
        return (async () => {
        const response = closingResponses.request();
        const result = new Promise<Write>((resolve) => settled.set(id, { resolve, response }));
        try {
          // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.4 — capture replay eligibility with the synchronous journal entry; recovery must also defer local refusals to the server.
          const admitted = await receiptCall(request); // Committed admission survives media/host housekeeping failure.
          release();
          if (admitted.denied) {
            settled.delete(id);
            const why = waiting && ["E_GENERATION", "E_PREDICT", "E_STORE_REPLACED", "E_DEV_RESET", "E_STORE_RESTORE", "E_ADMISSION_STORE"].includes(admitted.denied.code) ? identityRefusal(false) : admitted.denied;
            diagnose(why);
            const failed = { state: "failed" as const, id, why };
            return admitted.denied.code === "E_WRITE_ID_REUSE" ? failed : emitLocalFailure(failed);
          }
          const entry = admitted.ok as Queued;
          clock.raiseIssued(entry.now ?? 0);
          if (!entry.predictable && !entry.predicted.length) Object.assign(pending, { reason: !acquired
            ? "queued without a row: not acquired yet"
            : "queued without a row: local data does not justify prediction" });
          rerunTables(predictedTables(entry.predicted));
        } catch (error) {
          release(); settled.delete(id);
          const why = { code: "E_STORE", family: "store", message: `write ${id} admission outcome is unknown: ${String(error)}` };
          diagnose(why);
          return emitLocalFailure({ state: "failed", id, why });
        }
        if (closed) {
          try {
            await closingResponses.done;
            return response.write(await result as Write<R>);
          } finally { settled.delete(id); }
        }
        void flush();
        const wait = mutationWaitSeconds(options?.waitSeconds) ?? 4;
        if (link !== "online" || wait === 0) { settled.delete(id); return pending; }
        let timer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | undefined;
        try {
          const outcome = await Promise.race([result, new Promise<Write>((resolve) => { timer = setTimeout(() => resolve(pending), wait * 1_000); })]) as Write<R>;
          if (outcome.state === "pending" && closed) { await closingResponses.done; return response.write(outcome); }
          return outcome;
        }
        finally { clearTimeout(timer); settled.delete(id); }
        })();
        };
        if (!waiting) return submit();
        return identification.run<R>({ id, op: op.name, args }, async () => {
          const state = (await call({ op: "sync_state", capture: false })).ok as StoreCapture | undefined;
          const ready = identity!.check(state, backend, generation, unavailable() ?? partitionDenied);
          if (ready === true) { storeIdentity = state!.store_id!; identified = true; }
          return ready;
        }, submit, why => { diagnose(why); return emitLocalFailure({ state: "failed", id, why }); });
      })));
    }),
    write(id) { return unavailable() ? Promise.resolve(undefined) : identification.write(id) ?? lookupWrite(history, queued, id, unavailable, () => acquired); },
    pendingWrites: async () => identification.visible(await localPendingWrites(queued, history, () => unavailable() ?? (partitionDenied?.code === "E_STORE_RESTORE" ? partitionDenied : null), () => acquired, holds), !unavailable()),
    onPendingWrites: pending.subscribe,
    get acquired() { return acquired; },
    onAcquired(listener) { acquiredListeners.add(listener); return () => acquiredListeners.delete(listener); },
    upload: immediateUpload({ get refreshing() { return refreshing; }, unavailable, get partitionDenied() { return partitionDenied; }, get link() { return link; },

 ## 1. Default verdict
@@ -313,3 +313,64 @@ found late-init metadata preservation. Generated-leaf preflight and restoration
 were added. The final start/initializer delta is READY from both

+
+## 9. Wave-34 refusal and install calls (CALLS1)
+
+The wave-34 decomposition attributes the remaining Codex input gap primarily
+to extra model calls; nullable Quarry errors, image/file option confusion and
+install polling are product-fixable sources. Muse acceptance assertions often
+bury backend refusals. This increment changes diagnostics and tooling, not
+rules or runtime language semantics.
+
+`check --fix` applies only compiler-verified source spans. An optional Boolean
+condition becomes an explicit comparison with true; null does not admit it.
+Stable nullable arithmetic propagates null when the surrounding declaration
+accepts that result. Positive require/if comparisons may instead guard the
+optional operands before evaluating the original condition; an absent value
+cannot satisfy the guard. Required insert columns may be filled only from
+same-named declared arguments that pass the real binder in context. Case-only
+parameter spelling repairs and a fully pinned, unique, non-null population's
+`first 1` are similarly checked. No default zero/now, missing owner/role,
+invented page size, schema cap, rule or invariant is introduced. Calls are not
+duplicated by nullable arithmetic repairs. Unproved rewrites remain advice.
+The CLI rechecks every compiled source before each write, prints a source diff,
+recompiles after each edit and leaves a second run unchanged. Diagnostics show
+the exact edited line for the new single-line repairs.
+
+Proof failure content lives in `proofFailure`, callable by runner summaries.
+Proof-enabled SDK transports retain at most 32 refusal records containing only
+operation, role, code and field identifiers. Credentials, arguments, messages
+and row contents are not retained. A roster maps an active token to its role
+without printing it. Browser network replies and local admission refusals feed
+the same formatter. Uncaught starter failures print its content before Node's
+original assertion/stack/diff; browser callbacks report it before cleanup.
+Assertions retain their identity, class and comparison fields. Exact decoded
+refusal objects correlate with their request through a weak map. Code equality
+alone never establishes request identity; without that correlation, a recent
+observed request is explicitly labelled as context. Existing npm acceptance,
+frontend:proof and client:calls scripts activate reporting even if they import
+only the Node client. Device admission refusals retain their known operation
+and the available persona or roster role. If no request refusal exists,
+the nearest server refusal/error is named from the owned private log, without
+printing raw server text. Missing operation/role/field evidence is stated rather
+than invented. Runners retain ownership of output layout and budgets.
+
+File upload options on a schema without file columns receive one narrowly
+bounded retry after the explicit pre-body no-file-column refusal. The original
+bytes then pass the existing media sniffer, image/video schema bounds and
+normal authorization/placement checks. A mixed/file schema keeps explicit file
+semantics. No transport ambiguity or post-staging refusal authorizes this
+fallback; durable upload intents retain their immutable declared kind. Finite
+security campaign output calls file scenarios inapplicable on image-only
+schemas rather than displaying their expected refusal as a product red.
+
+The first install remains `npm i snapback4`, exposing the guide in seconds.
+Init prints the editable file map and reading route, then owns one blocking
+frontend install. A fresh Codex replay showed tool cleanup reclaiming the
+previous unjoined background owner before its first receipt; foreground
+ownership removes that race. npm chatter stays in the private log, automatic
+audit/funding chatter is deferred to the separate qualification path, and
+completion preserves npm's exit status. An explicit `--no-install` still defers
+installation; `node scripts/install.mjs --wait` joins a separately started
+installer with one final line. The script's explicit background mode retains
+request identity, IPC custody, owner-death cleanup and late-receipt checks.

import { observeRefusal, refusalObservation } from "./proof-observation.ts";
import { operationTables, type BackendJson } from "./backend.ts";
import { mintId } from "./client.ts";
import type { Queued } from "./local-types.ts";
import type { Refusal, Write } from "./contract.ts";
import type { DeviceCore } from "./local-device.ts";
import type { SessionStore } from "./auth.ts";

/** Internal synchronous web admission boundary. Native hosts still commit
 * through their normal async call. No transport runs before that commit. */
export const admissionCapture = Symbol("snapback4.admissionCapture");
export const admissionHost = (core: DeviceCore, capture: (request: Record<string, unknown>) => Refusal | undefined) => { core[admissionCapture] = capture; };
export const admitNow = (core: DeviceCore, request: Record<string, unknown>) => {
  const capture = core[admissionCapture];
  return capture ? capture(request) : browserAdmission() ? admissionRefusal("the device has no synchronous admission journal", "client.mutate", "open a webDevice() with an exclusive persistence claim before writing") : undefined;
};
export const browserAdmission = () => typeof window !== "undefined" && typeof window.location !== "undefined";
export const BROWSER_WRITE_FIX = "createApp({ url, device: webDevice(), sessionStore: localStorageSessionStore() })";
/** A browser app whose schema syncs no table (LLP 3000.013.000). */
export const ONLINE_WRITE_FIX = "createApp({ url, sessionStore: localStorageSessionStore() })";
let sdkClients = 0;
/** Clients createApp opens for its own tiers are not the bare browser client
 * that createClient's construction warning is about. */
export function sdkOwned<T>(create: () => T): T {
  sdkClients++;
  try { return create(); } finally { sdkClients--; }
}
export const sdkOwning = () => sdkClients > 0;
export function admissionRefusal(message: string, site = "client.mutate", rewrite = "wait for app.ready, restore writable localStorage, then retry with the same id"): Refusal {
  return { code: "E_ADMISSION", family: "store", message, site, rewrite, guide: "writes" };
}
/** A fulfilled failed promise also exposes its refusal in the calling stack.
 * Awaiting it remains the existing Write API. It has admitted no new intent. */
export function failedAdmission<R>(id: string, why: Refusal): Promise<Write<R>> & Extract<Write<R>, { state: "failed" }> {
  if (!refusalObservation(why)) observeRefusal(why.site ?? "client.mutate", "role unavailable", why);
  const write = { state: "failed" as const, id: typeof id === "string" ? id : "", why };
  return Object.assign(Promise.resolve(write), write);
}
const readers = new WeakMap<SessionStore["get"], (key: string) => string | null>();
export function synchronousSessionStore(store: SessionStore, read: (key: string) => string | null): SessionStore { readers.set(store.get, read); return store; }
export const sessionReader = (store: SessionStore) => readers.get(store.get);

/** Ordinary admission inputs, minted only after the identity wait completes. */
export function mutationEntry(id: string, seq: number, op: string, args: unknown, viewer: string,
  now: number, backend: BackendJson, replay_candidate: boolean): Queued {
  const new_ids = Array.from({ length: 8 }, () => mintId());
  const schema = backend.schema, program = backend.programs.find(p => p.name === op);
  const predictable = Boolean(program && schema && [...operationTables(program, schema)].every(t => schema.tables[t]?.sync));
  return { id, seq, op, args, viewer, now, new_ids, predictable, predicted: [], replay_candidate };
}

export function validEngine(value: unknown): value is DeviceEngine {
  const d = value as DeviceEngine | null;
  return !!d && typeof d === "object" && d.format === 1 && typeof d.version === "string" && /^\d+\.\d+\.\d+$/.test(d.version)
    && Array.isArray(d.capabilities) && d.capabilities.length <= 256 && d.capabilities.every(c => typeof c === "string" && c.length > 0 && c.length <= 128);
}
export function normalizeEngine(value: unknown): DeviceEngine { return validEngine(value) ? value : unknownEngine(); }

export function deviceVersionState(engine: DeviceEngine, onStatus?: (status: DeviceVersionStatus) => void) {
  let status: DeviceVersionStatus = { state: "compatible", engine };
  let publish: ((why: Refusal) => void) | undefined;
  const reads = new AbortController();
  const refuse = (why: Refusal | undefined) => {
    const error = deviceVersionRefusal(why);
    if (error) { status = error.status; reads.abort(error); publish?.(error.why); onStatus?.(status); }
    return error;
  };
  return {
    status: () => status,
    signal: reads.signal,
    blocked: () => status.state === "incompatible",

codex
**NOT READY — one material finding.**

- **P2: Shared local refusals can falsely identify a later operation as the exact failing request.** [proof-observation.ts:26](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b35-calls1/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/proof-observation.ts:26) overwrites the association whenever the same refusal object is observed again. [local.ts:1290](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b35-calls1/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/local.ts:1290) does this for persistent device/version, authentication, and restore refusals. If operations A and B share that refusal, an assertion containing A’s returned write reports B **without** the “nearest observed request” qualifier. A tiny in-memory probe confirmed this. This contradicts §9’s request-identity guarantee. Associate observations with individual returned results, or treat reused refusal objects as ambiguous; add a regression covering two operations sharing one refusal.

No other material findings. The final foreground installer behavior matches §9. Review remained read-only; no builds or full tests ran.
